3.4.2
5-dimensional, extremal, 3-charge BHs

Solving the EOMs

The solutions can be easily obtained as a limit of (
3.73
) for
\omega=0
. However, notice that it is possible to avoid the process of reconstructing the generating functions. In this case, only 3 unknown functions are necessary
[
1
]
. We have indeed

\mathcal{Z}_{0}=\mathcal{Z}_{h0}\,,\quad\mathcal{Z}_{+}\,,\quad\mathcal{Z}_{-}=\mathcal{Z}_{h-}\,,\quad W_{tt}=W_{rr}=1\,.

(3.75)

The Bianchi identity can be used to determine
\mathcal{Z}_{0}
. The KR equation is not automatically satisfied, but can be used to determine
\mathcal{Z}_{-}
. Finally, the Einstein equations and the dilaton equation form a system of linear differential equations for
\delta\mathcal{Z}_{+}
that can be solved directly.

The integration constants are determined demanding that the gauge charges do not receive
\alpha^{\prime}
corrections, namely that the numerator of the
1/r^{2}
term of the
\mathcal{Z}_{i}
is not modified, and that the asymptotic normalization of the fields is the same they have at
0^{\rm th}
-order.

Regular, extremal solutions

Explicitly, the three functions
\mathcal{Z}_{0}
,
\mathcal{Z}_{+}
, and
\mathcal{Z}_{-}
are

\displaystyle\mathcal{Z}_{0}

\displaystyle=1+\frac{q_{0}}{r^{2}}+\alpha^{\prime}\frac{q_{0}^{2}}{r^{2}(q_{0}+r^{2})^{2}}\,,

(3.76a)

\displaystyle\mathcal{Z}_{+}

\displaystyle=1+\frac{q_{+}}{r^{2}}-\alpha^{\prime}(1+\beta_{+}\beta_{-})\frac{q_{+}q_{-}}{r^{2}(q_{0}+r^{2})(q_{-}+r^{2})}\,,

(3.76b)

\displaystyle\mathcal{Z}_{-}

\displaystyle=1+\frac{q_{-}}{r^{2}}\,.

(3.76c)

where we used that, for
\omega=0

\beta_{+}=s_{+}\,,\quad\beta_{-}=s_{-}\,,\quad\beta_{0}=s_{0}\,.

(3.77)

Supersymmetry

The ansatz (
3.26
) takes the form (
3.35
) in the extremal case. Let us determine the unbroken supersymmetries of the field configurations for arbitrary choices of the functions
\mathcal{Z}_{0},\mathcal{Z}_{+},\mathcal{Z}_{-}
.
15
15

15

See also
Ref.
[
110
]
.
We use the Zehnbein basis

\displaystyle{\hat{e}}^{0}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{\mathcal{Z}_{+}\mathcal{Z}_{-}}}dt\,,\hskip 19.91684pt{\hat{e}}^{1}=\sqrt{\mathcal{Z}_{0}}dr\,,\hskip 19.91684pt{\hat{e}}^{i+1}=\sqrt{\mathcal{Z}_{0}}r/2v^{i}\,,

(3.78)

\displaystyle{\hat{e}}^{5}

\displaystyle=k_{\infty}\sqrt{\frac{\mathcal{Z}_{+}}{\mathcal{Z}_{-}}}\left[dz+\beta_{+}k_{\infty}^{-1}\left(\mathcal{Z}^{-1}_{+}-1\right)dt\right]\,,\hskip 19.91684pt{\hat{e}}^{m}=dy^{m}\,,

where the
v^{i}
,
i=1,2,3
are the SU
(2)
left-invariant Maurer-Cartan
1-forms, satisfying the Maurer-Cartan equation

dv^{i}=-\tfrac{1}{2}\epsilon^{ijk}v^{j}\wedge v^{k}\,.\hskip 28.45274pt\epsilon^{123}=+1\,,

(3.79)

Plugging this configuration into the Killing spinor equations

\delta_{\epsilon}\psi_{a}=0
,
\delta_{\epsilon}\lambda=0
and

\delta_{\epsilon}\chi^{A}=0
with the supersymmetry variations in
Eqs. (
3.13
)-(
3.15
), we immediately see that the
third of them (the gaugini’s) is automatically satisfied. It is not hard to
see that the second (the dilatini) is satisfied for supersymmetry parameters
satisfying the two (compatible) conditions

\displaystyle\tfrac{1}{2}\left(1-\beta_{-}\Gamma^{05}\right)\epsilon

\displaystyle=0\,,

(3.80a)

\displaystyle\tfrac{1}{2}\left(1+\beta_{0}\Gamma^{1234}\right)\epsilon

\displaystyle=0\,.

(3.80b)

Solving
\delta_{\epsilon}\psi_{0}=0
with a time-independent spinor, though,
demands
\beta_{-}=\beta_{+}
, and, using this condition in

\delta_{\epsilon}\psi_{1}=0
, we find that

\epsilon=(\mathcal{Z}_{+}\mathcal{Z}_{-})^{-1/4}\epsilon_{0}\,,

(3.81)

where
\epsilon_{0}
is an
r-
independent spinor that satisfies the above conditions.

The equations
\delta_{\epsilon}\psi_{i}=0
with
i=2,3,4
(the directions in
the 3-sphere) take the form

\left(v_{i}-\tfrac{1}{4}(1+\beta_{0})\Gamma^{i1}\right)\epsilon_{0}=0\,,

(3.82)

where
v_{i}
are the vectors dual to the left-invariant Maurer-Cartan 1-forms
in SU
(2)
(S
3
),
v^{i}
, defined above in Eq. (
3.79
).
When
\beta_{0}=-1
,
\epsilon_{0}
is just a constant spinor. When

\beta_{0}=+1
, we can rewrite the equation in
the form

\left(d-v^{i}T_{i}\right)\epsilon_{0}=0\,,

(3.83)

where we have defined the SU
(2)
generators

T_{i}\equiv\tfrac{1}{2}\Gamma^{i1}\,.

(3.84)

Indeed, it can be checked that they satisfy the commutation relations

[T_{i},T_{j}]=-\varepsilon_{ijk}T_{k}\,,

(3.85)

in the subspace of spinors satisfying Eq. (
3.80b
) with

\beta_{0}=+1
. Since, by definition,
16
16

16

Here we are using the
conventions and results of Ref.
[
133
]
.

v^{i}T_{i}=-u^{-1}du
, where
u
is a generic element of SU
(2)
,
Eq. (
3.83
) is equivalent to

d(u\epsilon_{0})=0\,,\,\,\,\,\,\Rightarrow\,\,\,\,\,\epsilon_{0}=u^{-1}\epsilon_{00}\,,

(3.86)

where
\epsilon_{00}
may, at most, depend on
z
. However,

\delta_{\epsilon}\psi_{5}=0
is solved for
z
-independent
\epsilon_{00}

upon use of the projector Eq. (
3.80a
) with

\beta_{-}=\beta_{+}
, and the rest of the Killing spinor equations,

\delta_{\epsilon}\psi_{m}=0
, are trivially solved for
y^{m}
-independent
(
i.e.
constant)
\epsilon_{00}
.

The conclusion is that the field configurations with
\beta_{-}=\beta_{+}

(and arbitrary
\beta_{0}=\pm 1
) are the only supersymmetric ones, although the Killing
spinors are quite different for
\beta_{0}=+1
and
\beta_{0}=-1
cases. This
result is true regardless of the values of the functions

\mathcal{Z}_{0},\mathcal{Z}_{+},\mathcal{Z}_{-}
which means that the

\alpha^{\prime}
corrections preserve the unbroken
supersymmetries of the zeroth-order solution.

3.4.3
5-dimensional, multi-center, 3-charge BHs

Solving the EOMs

We are going to solve the EOMs replacing explicitly the ansatz (
3.35
). The details of the computations can be found in
[
110
,
2
]
. Some intermediate steps are collected in appendix
C.2
. We assume that the
\mathcal{Z}_{i}
functions depend only on the coordinates of the 4-dimensional Euclidean space
\mathbb{E}^{4}
and that the
\beta_{i}
are signs. After some algebra we obtain that the Bianchi identity (
3.12
) is equivalent to the condition

\Delta_{(4)}\left\{\mathcal{Z}_{0}-\frac{\alpha^{\prime}}{4}\left(\frac{\partial_{m}\mathcal{Z}_{0}\,\partial^{m}\mathcal{Z}_{0}}{\mathcal{Z}_{0}^{2}}\right)\right\}=0\,,

(3.87)

where
\Delta_{(4)}
represents the Laplacian operator on
\mathbb{E}^{4}
and
\partial_{m}
are the derivatives dual to the vierbeins
v^{m}
. It is solved by

\mathcal{Z}_{0}=\mathcal{Z}_{0}^{(0)}+\frac{\alpha^{\prime}}{4}\left(\partial\log\mathcal{Z}_{0}^{(0)}\right)^{2}\,,

(3.88)

where
\mathcal{Z}_{0}^{(0)}
is any harmonic of
\mathbb{E}^{4}
. The KR equation of motion (
3.11c
) is in its turn equivalent to

\Delta_{(4)}\mathcal{Z}_{-}=0\,.

(3.89)

We conclude that
\mathcal{Z}_{-}
must be a harmonic function on
\mathbb{E}^{4}
. Finally, we have a single independent EOM among the Einstein equations (
3.11a
) and the dilaton EOM (
3.11b
). In particular, using the vielbein basis (
C.10
) introduced in appendix
C.2
, the
++
component of Einstein equations reduces to

\Delta_{(4)}\left\{\mathcal{Z}_{+}+\alpha^{\prime}\frac{(1+\beta_{+}\beta_{-})}{4}\left(\frac{\partial_{m}\mathcal{Z}_{+}\,\partial^{m}\mathcal{Z}_{-}}{\mathcal{Z}_{0}\mathcal{Z}_{-}}\right)\right\}=0\,,

(3.90)

which is solved by

\mathcal{Z}_{+}=\mathcal{Z}_{+}^{(0)}-\alpha^{\prime}\frac{(1+\beta_{+}\beta_{-})}{4}\left(\frac{\partial_{m}\mathcal{Z}_{+}^{(0)}\,\partial^{m}\mathcal{Z}_{-}}{\mathcal{Z}_{0}^{(0)}\mathcal{Z}_{-}}\right)\,,

(3.91)

where
\mathcal{Z}_{+}^{(0)}
is any harmonic of
\mathbb{E}^{4}
.

Regular solutions

Notice that the equations (
3.87
), (
3.89
) and (
3.90
) determine the
\mathcal{Z}_{i}
up to harmonic functions. These harmonic functions can be determined requiring regularity at the horizon, finding the proper asymptotic normalization of the fields and selecting the microcanonical ensemble. With the ansatz for the
0^{\rm th}
-order part of
\mathcal{Z}_{i}
given by (
3.36
) we obtain explicitly

\displaystyle\mathcal{Z}_{+}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{Z}_{+}^{(0)}-\alpha^{\prime}\left[(1+\beta_{+}\beta_{-})\,\mathcal{Z}_{-}^{-1}\mathcal{Z}_{0}^{(0)\,-1}\sum_{a,b}\frac{q^{a}_{+}q^{b}_{-}n^{m}_{a}n^{m}_{b}}{r^{3}_{a}r^{3}_{b}}\right]\,,

(3.92a)

\displaystyle\mathcal{Z}_{0}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{Z}_{0}^{(0)}+\alpha^{\prime}\left[\mathcal{Z}_{0}^{(0)\,-2}\sum_{a,b}\frac{q^{a}_{0}q^{b}_{0}n^{m}_{a}n^{m}_{b}}{r^{3}_{a}r^{3}_{b}}\right]\,,

(3.92b)

\displaystyle\mathcal{Z}_{-}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{Z}_{-}^{(0)}\,,

(3.92c)

where we have defined the unit radial vectors

n^{m}_{a}\equiv(x^{m}-x^{m}_{a})/r_{a}\,.

(3.93)

3.4.4
4-dimensional, non-extremal, 4-charge BHs

Solving the EOMs

A possible approach to solve the EOMs would be the method of generating function reconstruction presented in section
3.4.1
. Despite there being no obstruction to its application, all attempts to obtain an explicit solution with 4 independent parameters
q_{i}
failed. We focus therefore to the particular case with 3 independent charges. Therefore, we have

q_{0}=q_{\mathcal{H}}=q\,,\qquad q_{-}\,,\qquad q_{+}\,.

(3.94)

Replacing the ansatz (
3.47
) into the EOMs and the Bianchi identity of HST we can easily determine some of the unknown functions. The KR equation (
3.11c
) is automatically satisfied and has been used to determine the expression (
3.47c
) of the dilaton
\hat{\phi}
. Expanding the Bianchi identity (
3.12
) in
\alpha^{\prime}
and dropping
\mathcal{O}(\alpha^{\prime}{}^{2})
terms we obtain a second-order differential equation for
\delta\mathcal{Z}_{h0}
. It can easily be solved providing

\begin{split}\delta\mathcal{Z}_{h0}=&(1+s_{0}s_{\mathcal{H}})\left[\frac{q^{4}+q^{3}\omega+11q^{2}r\omega+15qr^{2}\omega+6r^{3}\omega}{4q^{2}r(q+r)^{3}}-\frac{3\omega}{2q^{3}}\log\left(\frac{q+r}{r}\right)\right]+\frac{d_{h0}^{(1)}}{r}\,,\end{split}

(3.95)

where
d_{h0}^{(1)}
is an integration constant (the second integration constant has been already fixed asking that the asymptotic value of
\mathcal{Z}_{h0}
is not modified). Imposing that the charge associated with
C^{(1)}_{w}
is not renormalized we obtain
d_{h0}^{(1)}=0
. The dilaton and Einstein equations form instead a complicate system of coupled differential equations. Once we replace our ansatz together with the expressions for
\hat{\phi}
and
\delta\mathcal{Z}_{h0}
and we drop
\mathcal{O}(\alpha^{\prime}{}^{2})
terms we obtain a total of 9 non-trivial equations (we indicate with
\mathbb{E}_{\hat{\mu}\hat{\nu}}
the components of the Einstein equations (
3.11a
) and with
\mathbb{E}_{\phi}
the dilaton EOM (
3.11b
))

\{\mathbb{E}_{tt},\mathbb{E}_{rr},\mathbb{E}_{\theta\theta},\mathbb{E}_{\varphi\varphi},\mathbb{E}_{ww},\mathbb{E}_{zz},\mathbb{E}_{tz},\mathbb{E}_{\varphi w},\mathbb{E}_{\phi}\}\,.

(3.96)

However, not all of them are independent. For instance, we can drop
\mathbb{E}_{\varphi\varphi}
and
\mathbb{E}_{\varphi w}
because they are combinations of the other EOMs.
\mathbb{E}_{ww}
turns out to be a second-order differential equation for the combination
\delta\mathcal{Z}_{\mathcal{H}}-\delta\mathcal{Z}_{0}
. Solving it, we obtain

\begin{split}\delta\mathcal{Z}_{\mathcal{H}}=&\;\delta\mathcal{Z}_{0}+(d_{0}^{(1)}-d_{\mathcal{H}}^{(1)})\left[\frac{4q(q-\omega)}{r\omega^{2}}-\frac{(2q-\omega)(2qr+qw-r\omega)}{r\omega^{3}}\log\left(1+\frac{\omega}{r}\right)\right]\\
&-\frac{q(1+s_{0}s_{\mathcal{H}})(q-\omega)}{4r(q+r)^{3}}\,,\end{split}

(3.97)

where we have imposed that both
\mathcal{Z}_{\mathcal{H}}
and
\mathcal{Z}_{0}
vanish asymptotically.
d_{0}^{(1)}
and
d_{\mathcal{H}}^{(1)}
are integration constants and represent the poles of the
1/r
terms of
\delta\mathcal{Z}_{0}
and
\delta\mathcal{Z}_{\mathcal{H}}
.
\mathbb{E}_{\theta\theta}
is an algebraic constraint for
\delta W_{rr}
. We can use it to determine
\delta W_{rr}
as a function of the other unknown functions and their derivatives (we omit at this stage the actual expression because of its lengthiness)

\delta W_{rr}=f\left(r,\delta W_{tt},\delta W_{tt}^{\prime},\delta\mathcal{Z}_{0},\delta\mathcal{Z}_{0}^{\prime},\delta\mathcal{Z}_{0}^{\prime\prime},\delta\mathcal{Z}_{-},\delta\mathcal{Z}_{-}^{\prime},\delta\mathcal{Z}_{h-},\delta\mathcal{Z}_{h-}^{\prime},\delta\mathcal{Z}_{h-}^{\prime\prime}\right)\,.

(3.98)

We are left with 5 equations and 5 unknown functions. Despite the complexity of the system is possible to solve it with the procedure of
[
4
]
. First, we consider an ansatz for the unknown functions with arbitrary coefficients

\delta\mathcal{Z}_{i}=\sum_{k>0}\frac{d_{i}^{(k)}}{r^{k}}\,,\qquad\delta\mathcal{Z}_{h-}=\sum_{k>0}\frac{d_{h-}^{(k)}}{r^{k}}\,,\qquad\delta W_{j}=\sum_{k>0}\frac{d_{wj}^{(k)}}{r^{k}}\,,

(3.99)

where we have only assumed that the
\alpha^{\prime}
corrections do not modify the asymptotic value of the
\mathcal{Z}
and
W
functions. Then, we replace the series expansions into the EOMs and we demand that they are solved order by order in powers of of
1/r
. In this way, we obtain a set of algebraic equations for the coefficients
d^{(k)}
for arbitrarily large values of
k
. Solving such equations we obtain the asymptotic expansion of the unknown functions in powers of
1/r
. The coefficients of one of these functions are not determined, signaling that only 4 of the 5 EOMs left are truly independent. More precisely, we find a family of solutions which depend on the coefficients

\left\{d_{h-}^{(1)},d_{-}^{(1)},d_{+}^{(1)},d_{\mathcal{H}}^{(1)},d_{0}^{(1)},d_{wt}^{(1)},d_{wr}^{(1)},d_{h-}^{(k)}\right\}\,,\qquad k\geq 3\,.

(3.100)

Imposing that the charges associated with
A^{z}
and
C_{z}^{(1)}
are not renormalized (i.e. they do not receive
\alpha^{\prime}
corrections) we fix
d_{h-}^{(1)}=d_{+}^{(1)}=0
. Then, we set to zero the coefficients
d_{h-}^{(k)}
with
k\geq 3
. This can always be done without loss of generality because it is equivalent to performing a proper change of coordinates. We obtain

\delta\mathcal{Z}_{h-}=\frac{1}{2}\left(d_{wt}^{(1)}-d_{wr}^{(1)}-2d_{-}^{(1)}\right)\frac{q_{-}}{r^{2}}\,.

(3.101)

With this expression we can easily determine some other quantities. First of all we notice that expanding the dilaton in series we get

e^{-2\phi}\sim\frac{c_{\phi}}{q_{-}}+\mathcal{O}(1/r)\,,

(3.102)

which fixes
c_{\phi}=e^{-2\phi_{\infty}}q_{-}
. Second, we can use the compatibility with the T-duality constraints to extract
\mathcal{Z}_{\pm}
(see appendix
C.3
for more details). The action of T-duality along the
z
direction on the lower dimensional fields is

T_{z}:\qquad C^{(1)}_{z}\leftrightarrow A^{z}\,,\qquad k\leftrightarrow 1/k^{(1)}\,,\qquad ds^{2}_{E}\leftrightarrow ds^{2}_{E}\,,\qquad e^{-2\phi}\leftrightarrow e^{-2\phi}\,.\\

(3.103)

Assuming that
T_{z}
can be implemented by

T_{z}:\qquad q_{+}\leftrightarrow q_{-}\,,\qquad\beta_{+}\leftrightarrow\beta_{-}\,,\qquad k_{\infty}\leftrightarrow 1/k_{\infty}\,,

(3.104)

we obtain (
C.28
), which gives

\delta\mathcal{Z}_{-}=\delta\mathcal{Z}_{h-}+\mathcal{Z}_{-}\left[\Delta_{k}-\frac{\Delta_{C}}{\beta_{+}}+\frac{r^{2}}{2}\left(\frac{\delta\mathcal{Z}_{h-}^{\prime}}{q_{-}}-\frac{T_{z}[\delta\mathcal{Z}_{h-}^{\prime}]}{q_{+}}\right)\right]\,,

(3.105)

where
T_{z}
is the operator implementing the T-duality transformation. This assumption imposes non-trivial constraints on some of the integration constants appearing in the series describing the expansion of
\delta\mathcal{Z}_{-}
. Indeed, the series
\{d^{(k)}_{-}\}
satisfies (
3.105
) provided that

T_{z}\left[d_{h-}^{(2)}\right]=\frac{1}{2}q_{+}\left(d_{wt}^{(1)}-d_{wr}^{(1)}\right)\,,\qquad d_{-}^{(1)}=\frac{(q_{+}-q_{-})}{2q_{-}-w}d_{wt}^{(1)}\,.

(3.106)

Expanding equation (
C.25
) we obtain an expression for
\delta\mathcal{Z}_{+}
which matches the series
\{d_{+}^{(k)}\}
without further constraints

\delta\mathcal{Z}_{+}=T_{z}\left[\delta\mathcal{Z}_{h-}\right]-\mathcal{Z}_{+}\frac{\Delta_{C}}{\beta_{+}}\,.

(3.107)

Once we replace the expressions obtained for
\delta\mathcal{Z}_{-}
,
\delta\mathcal{Z}_{+}
,
\delta\mathcal{Z}_{\mathcal{H}}
,
\delta\mathcal{Z}_{h-}
and
\delta W_{rr}
into the EOMs we obtain a set of differential equations for
\delta W_{tt}
and
\delta\mathcal{Z}_{0}
. In particular,
\mathbb{E}_{zz}
is a first-order differential equations which involves only
\delta W_{tt}
. Solving it we obtain

\begin{split}\delta W_{tt}=\quad&\frac{r\omega^{2}-q_{-}\omega(2r+\omega)}{8r^{2}(q+r)^{2}(q_{-}+r)}-\frac{\beta_{-}\beta_{+}q_{-}q_{+}\omega(r+\omega)}{4r^{2}(q+r)^{2}(q_{-}+r)(q_{+}-\omega)}\\
&+\frac{d_{wt}^{(1)}(2q_{+}+2r+\omega)}{2r^{2}}-\frac{d_{wr}^{(1)}\omega}{2r^{2}}\,.\end{split}

(3.108)

Replacing this expression for
\delta W_{tt}
into the EOMs, we are left with a fourth-order differential equation for
\delta\mathcal{Z}_{0}
. Instead of solving it directly, we focus on finding the generating function of the coefficients
\{d_{0}^{(k)}\}
. Replacing the guessed generating function into the fourth-order order differential equation we can verify that it is exactly solved and we actually find the expression of
\delta\mathcal{Z}_{0}
(we omit again the actual expression because of its lengthiness). With the explicit expression of
\delta\mathcal{Z}_{0}
one can finally reconstruct the explicit expressions of all the
\delta\mathcal{Z}
s and
\delta W
s. It is then possible to verify that the expressions obtained solve exactly all the EOMs of the HST effective action at first-order in
\alpha^{\prime}
.

Regularity conditions

At zeroth order the horizon lies at
r=-\omega
. At first order it may be shifted and placed at
r_{H}=-\omega+\alpha^{\prime}\delta r
. Therefore, we expand the 4d fields around
z=(r-r_{H})
. We obtain

\displaystyle e^{-2\phi}

\displaystyle\quad=\quad y^{(0)}_{\phi}+y^{(0,\log)}_{\phi}\log z+\mathcal{O}(z\log z)\,,

(3.109a)

\displaystyle k

\displaystyle\quad=\quad y^{0}_{k}+\mathcal{O}(z)\,,

(3.109b)

\displaystyle\ell

\displaystyle\quad=\quad y^{(0)}_{\ell}+y^{(0,\log)}_{\ell}\log z+\mathcal{O}(z\log z)\,,

(3.109c)

\displaystyle g_{tt,E}

\displaystyle\quad=\quad y^{(0)}_{tt}+y^{(1,\log)}_{tt}z\log z+\mathcal{O}(z)\,,

(3.109d)

\displaystyle g_{rr,E}

\displaystyle\quad=\quad\frac{y^{(-2)}_{rr}}{z^{2}}+\frac{y^{(-1)}_{rr}}{z}+\frac{y^{(-1,\log)}_{rr}}{z}\log z+y^{(0,\log)}_{rr}\log z+\mathcal{O}(1)\,,

(3.109e)

\displaystyle g_{\theta\theta,E}

\displaystyle\quad=\quad y^{(0)}_{\theta\theta}+\mathcal{O}(z\log z)\,,

(3.109f)

\displaystyle F^{w}_{\theta\varphi}

\displaystyle\quad=\quad y^{(0)}_{Fw}\,,

(3.109g)

\displaystyle F^{z}_{tr}

\displaystyle\quad=\quad y^{(0)}_{Fz}+\mathcal{O}(z)\,,

(3.109h)

\displaystyle G_{w}

\displaystyle\quad=\quad y^{(0)}_{Gw}\,,

(3.109i)

\displaystyle G_{z}

\displaystyle\quad=\quad y^{(0)}_{Gz}+\mathcal{O}(z)\,,

(3.109j)

where the
y_{i}^{(k)}
s are combinations of
q_{i}
,
\omega
,
\beta_{i}
,
d_{\mathcal{H}}^{(1)}
,
d_{0}^{(1)}
,
d_{wt}^{(1)}
,
d_{wr}^{(1)}
,
\delta r
,
\phi_{\infty}
,
k_{\infty}
,
\ell_{\infty}
and
\alpha^{\prime}
. Imposing that the BH horizon is placed at
z=0
, we obtain the condition

y^{(0)}_{tt}=0\,.

(3.110)

Imposing that the scalars have a finite value on the BH horizon, we get

y^{(0,\log)}_{\phi}=y^{(0,\log)}_{\ell}=0\,.

(3.111)

Demanding that the Hawking temperature is finite, we obtain

y^{(1,\log)}_{tt}=y^{(-2)}_{rr}=y^{(-1,\log)}_{rr}=0\,.

(3.112)

The conditions (
3.110
),(
3.111
),(
3.112
) together with the requirement that the BH horizon is not shifted,
i.e.

\delta r=0
, lead to

\displaystyle d_{0}^{(1)}

\displaystyle=d_{\mathcal{H}}^{(1)}\,,

(3.113a)

\displaystyle\begin{split}d_{wt}^{(1)}&=\frac{\omega(\omega-2q)}{(q-\omega)(\omega-2q_{+})}d_{\mathcal{H}}^{(1)}+\frac{s_{0}s_{\mathcal{H}}\,q\,\omega\left(2q^{2}-6q\omega+5\omega^{2}\right)}{20(q-\omega)^{4}(\omega-2q_{+})}\\
&\quad+\frac{\omega\left(-4q^{3}+22q^{2}\omega-35q\omega^{2}+25\omega^{3}\right)}{40(q-\omega)^{4}(\omega-2q_{+})}\,,\end{split}

(3.113b)

\displaystyle\begin{split}d_{wr}^{(1)}&=\frac{(2q-\omega)}{q-\omega}d_{\mathcal{H}}^{(1)}-\frac{s_{0}s_{\mathcal{H}}\,q\left(2q^{2}-6q\omega+5\omega^{2}\right)}{20(q-\omega)^{4}}\\
&\quad+\frac{4q^{3}-12q^{2}\omega+15q\omega^{2}-15\omega^{3}}{40(q-\omega)^{4}}\,.\end{split}

(3.113c)

Regular solutions

Identifying the physical mass
17
17

17

In this case we are not picking the microcanonical ensemble, but we are imposing that in the extremal limit we recover the extremal BHs mass. See
[
8
]
for more details.
we determine
d_{\mathcal{H}}^{(1)}
and we obtain the regular solution

\displaystyle\begin{split}\delta\mathcal{Z}_{h0}=&\;(1+s_{0}s_{\mathcal{H}})\left[\frac{q^{4}+q^{3}\omega+11q^{2}r\omega+15qr^{2}\omega+6r^{3}\omega}{4q^{2}r(q+r)^{3}}-\frac{3\omega}{2q^{3}}\log\mathcal{Z}_{0}\right]\,,\end{split}

(3.114a)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\delta\mathcal{Z}_{0}=&\;\frac{d_{\mathcal{H}}^{(1)}}{r}+\frac{\omega^{2}(2q-\omega)(s_{0}s_{\mathcal{H}}+4)(\omega q-3\omega r+2rq-2r^{2})}{40q^{3}r(q-\omega)^{3}}\log\mathcal{Z}_{0}\\
&+\frac{1}{120q^{2}r(q+r)^{3}(q-\omega)^{3}}\bigg[-qr^{2}(q-3\omega)\left(5q^{3}+57q\omega^{2}-26\omega^{3}\right)\\
&+q^{2}r\left(-16q^{4}+51q^{3}\omega-165q^{2}\omega^{2}+205q\omega^{3}-51\omega^{4}\right)\\
&+2q^{3}(q-\omega)\left(18q^{3}-58q^{2}\omega+41q\omega^{2}-19\omega^{3}\right)\\
&+24r^{4}\omega^{2}(2q-\omega)+36r^{3}\omega^{2}(2q-\omega)(q+\omega)\bigg]\\
&+\frac{s_{0}s_{\mathcal{H}}}{240q^{2}r(q+r)^{3}(q-\omega)^{3}}\bigg[q^{3}(q-\omega)\left(48q^{3}-128q^{2}\omega+91q\omega^{2}-29\omega^{3}\right)\\
&+qr^{2}\left(10q^{4}-30q^{3}\omega+9q^{2}\omega^{2}+86q\omega^{3}-39\omega^{4}\right)\\
&+2q^{2}r\left(16q^{4}-51q^{3}\omega+30q^{2}\omega^{2}+20q\omega^{3}-9\omega^{4}\right)\\
&+12r^{4}\omega^{2}(2q-\omega)+18r^{3}\omega^{2}(2q-\omega)(q+\omega)\bigg]\,,\end{split}

(3.114b)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\delta\mathcal{Z}_{\mathcal{H}}=&\;\delta\mathcal{Z}_{0}-\frac{q(1+s_{0}s_{\mathcal{H}})(q-\omega)}{4r(q+r)^{3}}\,,\end{split}

(3.114c)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\delta\mathcal{Z}_{h-}=&\;\frac{q_{-}(2q-\omega)(q_{-}-\omega)}{r^{2}(q-\omega)(\omega-2q_{-})}d_{\mathcal{H}}^{(1)}-\frac{qq_{-}\left(2q^{2}-6q\omega+5\omega^{2}\right)(q_{-}-\omega)(s_{0}s_{\mathcal{H}}-1)}{20r^{2}(q-\omega)^{4}(\omega-2q_{-})}\\
&+\frac{q_{-}\omega^{2}\left[q^{2}+q(q_{-}-3\omega)+\omega(4\omega-3q_{-})\right]}{8r^{2}(q-\omega)^{4}(\omega-2q_{-})}\,,\end{split}

(3.114d)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\delta\mathcal{Z}_{-}=&\;\delta\mathcal{Z}_{h-}+\mathcal{Z}_{-}\left[\Delta_{k}-\frac{\Delta_{C}}{\beta_{+}}+\frac{r^{2}}{2}\left(\frac{\delta\mathcal{Z}_{h-}^{\prime}}{q_{-}}-\frac{T_{z}[\delta\mathcal{Z}_{h-}^{\prime}]}{q_{+}}\right)\right]\,,\end{split}

(3.114e)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\delta\mathcal{Z}_{+}=&\;T_{z}\left[\delta\mathcal{Z}_{h-}\right]-\mathcal{Z}_{+}\frac{\Delta_{C}}{\beta_{+}}\,,\end{split}

(3.114f)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\delta W_{tt}=&\;\frac{q\omega\left(2q^{2}-6q\omega+5\omega^{2}\right)(r+\omega)(s_{0}s_{\mathcal{H}}-1)}{20r^{2}(q-\omega)^{4}(\omega-2q_{+})}-\frac{\omega(2q-\omega)(r+\omega)}{r^{2}(q-\omega)(\omega-2q_{+})}d_{\mathcal{H}}^{(1)}\\
&\frac{q^{2}\omega^{2}(2q_{+}+2r+\omega)}{8r^{2}(q-\omega)^{4}(\omega-2q_{+})}+\frac{\omega^{2}}{8r(q+r)^{2}(q_{-}+r)}\\
&+\frac{q_{-}\omega^{2}(2r+\omega)-q_{-}q_{+}\omega[2r(\beta_{-}\beta_{+}+1)+2\beta_{-}\beta_{+}\omega+\omega]}{8r^{2}(q+r)^{2}(q_{-}+r)(q_{+}-\omega)}\\
&+\frac{5\omega^{5}}{16r^{2}(q-\omega)^{4}(\omega-2q_{+})}+\frac{5q_{+}\omega^{4}}{8r^{2}(q-\omega)^{4}(\omega-2q_{+})}\\
&-\frac{q\omega^{3}(4q_{+}+5r+3\omega)}{8r^{2}(q-\omega)^{4}(\omega-2q_{+})}+\frac{5\omega^{4}}{8r(q-\omega)^{4}(\omega-2q_{+})}+\frac{3\omega^{4}}{16r^{2}(q-\omega)^{4}}\,,\end{split}

(3.114g)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\delta W_{rr}=&\;\frac{(2q-\omega)(r+\omega)}{r^{2}(q-\omega)}d_{\mathcal{H}}^{(1)}+\frac{\omega^{2}(2q-\omega)(r+\omega)(2r+\omega)(s_{0}s_{\mathcal{H}}+4)}{20q^{3}r(q-\omega)^{3}}\log\mathcal{Z}_{0}\\
&+\frac{(s_{0}s_{\mathcal{H}}-1)}{120q^{2}r^{2}(q+r)^{4}(q-\omega)^{4}}\bigg[-12r^{6}\omega^{2}\left(2q^{2}-3q\omega+\omega^{2}\right)\\
&-6q^{7}\omega\left(2q^{2}-6q\omega+5\omega^{2}\right)\\
&-6r^{5}\left(2q^{5}-6q^{4}\omega+19q^{3}\omega^{2}-15q^{2}\omega^{3}-2q\omega^{4}+3\omega^{5}\right)\\
&+r^{4}\left(-46q^{6}+124q^{5}\omega-177q^{4}\omega^{2}-5q^{3}\omega^{3}+125q^{2}\omega^{4}-45q\omega^{5}-6\omega^{6}\right)\\
&-qr^{3}\left(64q^{6}-136q^{5}\omega+42q^{4}\omega^{2}+221q^{3}\omega^{3}-167q^{2}\omega^{4}+15q\omega^{5}+21\omega^{6}\right)\\
&+q^{2}r^{2}\left(-48q^{6}+84q^{5}\omega+37q^{4}\omega^{2}-157q^{3}\omega^{3}-3q^{2}\omega^{4}+53q\omega^{5}-26\omega^{6}\right)\\
&+q^{3}r\left(-12q^{6}-12q^{5}\omega+118q^{4}\omega^{2}-145q^{3}\omega^{3}+21q^{2}\omega^{4}+5q\omega^{5}-5\omega^{6}\right)\bigg]\\
&+\frac{\omega^{2}}{8q^{2}r^{2}(q+r)^{2}(q-\omega)^{4}}\bigg[-4r^{4}\left(2q^{2}-3q\omega+\omega^{2}\right)\\
&-qr\left(q^{4}+9q^{2}\omega^{2}-7q\omega^{3}+3\omega^{4}\right)+r^{3}\left(-11q^{3}+3q^{2}\omega+12q\omega^{2}-6\omega^{3}\right)\\
&+q^{2}\omega\left(-q^{3}+2q^{2}\omega-4q\omega^{2}+\omega^{3}\right)\\
&+r^{2}\left(-2q^{4}-16q^{3}\omega+17q^{2}\omega^{2}-3q\omega^{3}-2\omega^{4}\right)\bigg]\,.\end{split}

(3.114h)

with

\displaystyle\begin{split}d_{\mathcal{H}}^{(1)}=\;&\frac{1}{40(q-\omega)^{3}(2q-\omega)D}\bigg\{-8q^{3}(s_{0}s_{\mathcal{H}}-1)(q(q_{-}+q_{+})+2q_{-}q_{+})\\
&+\omega^{3}[(q^{2}(48s_{0}s_{\mathcal{H}}-98)+q(q_{-}+q_{+})(54s_{0}s_{\mathcal{H}}-59)+4q_{-}q_{+}(8s_{0}s_{\mathcal{H}}+7)]\\
&-4q\omega^{2}[q^{2}(8s_{0}s_{\mathcal{H}}-13)+2q(q_{-}+q_{+})(8s_{0}s_{\mathcal{H}}-13)+q_{-}q_{+}(22s_{0}s_{\mathcal{H}}+13)]\\
&+4q^{2}\omega[2q^{2}(s_{0}s_{\mathcal{H}}-1)+q(q_{-}+q_{+})(9s_{0}s_{\mathcal{H}}-14)+2q_{-}q_{+}(8s_{0}s_{\mathcal{H}}-3)]\\
&+\omega^{4}(-16s_{0}s_{\mathcal{H}}(2q+q_{-}+q_{+})+72q+11(q_{-}+q_{+}))+2\omega^{5}(4s_{0}s_{\mathcal{H}}-9)\bigg\}\,,\end{split}

(3.115)

\displaystyle D=

\displaystyle\;-2q(q_{-}+q_{+}-\omega)+3\omega(q_{-}+q_{+})-4q_{-}q_{+}-2\omega^{2}\,,

(3.116)

and
T_{z}
,
T_{w}
are the T-duality operators

\displaystyle T_{z}:\qquad q_{+}\leftrightarrow q_{-}\,,\qquad\beta_{+}\leftrightarrow\beta_{-}\,,\qquad k_{\infty}\leftrightarrow 1/k_{\infty}\,,

(3.117a)

\displaystyle T_{w}:\qquad q\leftrightarrow q\,,\qquad\beta_{\mathcal{H}}\leftrightarrow\beta_{0}\,,\qquad\ell_{\infty}\leftrightarrow 1/\ell_{\infty}\,.

(3.117b)

It can be verified that, applying (
3.117
) to this solution, the ansatz (
3.47
) obeys the transformation rules

\displaystyle T_{z}:\qquad C^{(1)}_{z}\leftrightarrow A^{z}\,,\qquad k\leftrightarrow 1/k^{(1)}\,,\qquad ds^{2}_{E}\leftrightarrow ds^{2}_{E}\,,\qquad e^{-2\phi}\leftrightarrow e^{-2\phi}\,,

(3.118a)

\displaystyle T_{w}:\qquad C^{(1)}_{w}\leftrightarrow A^{w}\,,\qquad\ell\leftrightarrow 1/\ell^{(1)}\,,\qquad ds^{2}_{E}\leftrightarrow ds^{2}_{E}\,,\qquad e^{-2\phi}\leftrightarrow e^{-2\phi}\,.

(3.118b)

We conclude by plotting some of the curvature invariants (see figures

,
3.5

3.6
). They show explicitly that the solution obtained for a typical choice of charges and mass within the range of validity of the perturbative regime does not present curvature singularities outside the BH horizon. The plots do not extend beyond
r=0
because there is a logarithmic singularity at that point.

3.4.5
4-dimensional, extremal, 4-charge BHs

Solving the EOMs

We can easily obtain an extremal solution by taking the
\omega=0
limit of the regular solution presented in the previous section. However, this is not the most general result available. In the extremal limit it is possible to compute the corrections to the solutions with 4 independent charges.

According to
[
1
]
in the extremal case we can simplify ansatz (
3.47
). We only need 5 independent functions

\mathcal{Z}_{H}\,,\qquad\mathcal{Z}_{0}\,,\qquad\mathcal{Z}_{h0}\,,\qquad\mathcal{Z}_{+}\,,\qquad\mathcal{Z}_{-}=\mathcal{Z}_{h-}\,,\qquad W_{rr}=W_{tt}=1\,.

(3.119)

The Bianchi identity can be used to determine
\mathcal{Z}_{h0}
. The KR equation is automatically solved by our ansatz for the dilaton (
3.47c
). Finally, the dilaton and the Einstein equations form them a system of coupled linear differential equations for
\delta\mathcal{Z}_{\mathcal{H}}
,
\delta\mathcal{Z}_{0}
,
\delta\mathcal{Z}_{+}
and
\delta\mathcal{Z}_{-}
. This system can be solved directly, without using the method of the generating function reconstruction.

In order to determine the integration constants we demand that the 4 gauge charges maintain their
0^{\rm th}
-order expressions, which is equivalent to asking for the absence of the
1/r
term in the asymptotic expansion of
\delta\mathcal{Z}_{h0}
,
\delta\mathcal{Z}_{+}
and
\delta\mathcal{Z}_{-}
. We also demand that all the fields keep their asymptotic values. Finally we determine the corrections to
\delta\mathcal{Z}_{\mathcal{H}}
and
\delta\mathcal{Z}_{0}
by demanding that their
1/r
poles at
r=0
vanish. One can verify that this last two conditions are compatible with regularity at the horizon and they are equivalent to determine the corrections to the mass and the position of the event horizon.

Regular, extremal solutions

We can divide the solutions in two families
[
1
]
, depending on the value of
\beta_{\mathcal{H}}\beta_{0}
. In the two cases the form of the corrections changes significantly. In the case
\beta_{\mathcal{H}}\beta_{0}=1
, we get

\displaystyle\mathcal{Z}_{+}

\displaystyle=1+\frac{{q}_{+}}{r}-\frac{\alpha^{\prime}}{4}(\beta_{+}\beta_{-}+1)\frac{{q}_{+}{q}_{-}}{r(r+{q}_{0})(r+{q})(r+{q}_{-})}\,,

(3.120a)

\displaystyle\mathcal{Z}_{-}

\displaystyle=1+\frac{{q}_{-}}{r}\,,

(3.120b)

\displaystyle\mathcal{Z}_{0}

\displaystyle={\cal Z}_{0h}=1+\frac{{q}_{0}}{r}+\frac{\alpha^{\prime}}{4}\frac{{q}^{\,2}(r+{q}_{0})^{2}+{q}_{0}^{2}(r+{q})^{2}}{r(r+{q}_{0})^{2}(r+{q})^{3}}\,,

(3.120c)

\displaystyle\mathcal{Z}_{\mathcal{H}}

\displaystyle=1+\frac{{q}}{r}\,.

(3.120d)

In the case with
\beta_{0}\beta_{\mathcal{H}}=-1
, we obtain, instead,

\displaystyle\begin{split}\delta\mathcal{Z}_{0}&=\frac{1}{4(q_{0}-q)^{5}}\Bigg\{-\frac{q(q_{0}-q)^{5}}{(r+q)^{3}}+\frac{(q_{0}-q)^{4}(q+q_{0})}{(r+q)^{2}}+\frac{\left(q-q_{0}\right)^{4}q_{0}}{(r+q_{0})^{2}}\\[2.84526pt]
&+\frac{(2q_{0}-q)(q_{0}-q)^{3}}{r+q_{0}}-\frac{(q_{0}-q)^{3}(8q_{0}+q)}{r+q}-\frac{2q_{0}(2q^{3}+29q^{2}q_{0}+12qq_{0}^{2}-3q_{0}^{3})}{r}\\[2.84526pt]
&-\frac{4qq_{0}^{2}\left(q{}^{2}+3q_{0}\left(2q+q_{0}\right)\right)}{r^{2}}+\frac{2qq_{0}}{(q_{0}-q)r^{3}}\bigg\{qq_{0}\log{\left(\frac{q_{0}}{q}\right)}\bigg[3qr(q+12r)\\[2.84526pt]
&+q_{0}\left[2\left(q{}^{2}+18qr+12r^{2}\right)+3q_{0}\left(4q+7r+2q_{0}\right)\right]\bigg]\\[2.84526pt]
&-\log{\left(\frac{r+q_{0}}{r+q}\right)}\Big[9q{}^{2}r^{3}+3q_{0}qr\left(q{}^{2}+12qr+4r^{2}\right)\\[2.84526pt]
&+q_{0}^{2}\big[2q{}^{3}+36q{}^{2}r+24qr^{2}-r^{3}+3qq_{0}\left(4q+7r+2q_{0}\right)\big]\Big]\bigg\}\Bigg\}\,,\end{split}

(3.121a)

\displaystyle\delta\mathcal{Z}_{h0}

\displaystyle=\frac{(q_{0}-q)[q_{0}(r+q)+q(r+q_{0})]}{4(r+q)^{3}(r+q_{0})^{2}}\,,

(3.121b)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\delta\mathcal{H}&=\frac{1}{2(q_{0}-q)^{5}}\Bigg\{\frac{q}{r}\left(12q_{0}q{}^{2}+29q_{0}^{2}q+2q_{0}^{3}-3q^{3}\right)\\[2.84526pt]
&+\frac{2q_{0}q^{2}}{r^{2}}\left(6q_{0}q+q_{0}^{2}+3q{}^{2}\right)-\frac{q_{0}(q_{0}-q)^{3}}{r+q}-\frac{3q(q_{0}-q)^{3}}{r+q_{0}}\\[2.84526pt]
&+\frac{q_{0}q}{r^{3}(q_{0}-q)}\bigg[r^{3}\left(12q_{0}q+9q_{0}^{2}-q^{2}\right)\log{\left(\frac{r+q_{0}}{r+q}\right)}\\[2.84526pt]
&+q_{0}q\Big[12q_{0}\left(3r^{2}+3rq+q{}^{2}\right)+q_{0}^{2}(3r+2q)+3q\left(8r^{2}+7rq+2q{}^{2}\right)\Big]\times\\[2.84526pt]
&\times\left[\log{\left(\frac{r+q_{0}}{r+q}\right)}-\log{\left(\frac{q_{0}}{q}\right)}\right]\bigg]\Bigg\}\,,\end{split}

(3.121c)

\displaystyle\delta\mathcal{Z}_{+}

\displaystyle=\frac{q_{+}}{q_{-}}\delta\mathcal{Z}_{-}-(\beta_{+}\beta_{-}+1)\frac{q_{+}q_{-}}{4r(r+q_{-})(r+q_{0})(r+q)}\,,

(3.121d)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\delta\mathcal{Z}_{-}&=\frac{q_{-}}{4r^{3}(q_{0}-q)^{5}}\Bigg\{8q_{0}qr^{3}\log{\left(\frac{r+q_{0}}{r+q}\right)}\\[2.84526pt]
&+(q_{0}+q)\bigg[2q_{0}q(q_{0}q-3r^{2})\log{\left(\frac{1+r/q}{1+r/q_{0}}\right)}+r(q_{0}-q)\left[r(q_{0}+q)-2q_{0}q\right]\bigg]\Bigg\}\,.\end{split}

(3.121e)

The explicit form of the constant
\hat{c}_{\phi}
appearing in (
3.47c
) is

\hat{c}_{\phi}=e^{-2\hat{\phi}_{\infty}}q_{-}\left\{1-\frac{\alpha^{\prime}}{4}\Upsilon\right\}\,,

(3.122)

where we have defined
18
18

18

This quantity is proportional to the correction to the attractor value of the dilaton.

\Upsilon=\frac{1}{(q_{0}-q)^{5}}\left[6q_{0}q(q_{0}+q)\log{\left(\frac{q_{0}}{q}\right)}-(q_{0}-q)(q_{0}^{2}+q^{2}+10q_{0}q)\right]\,.

(3.123)

Supersymmetry

The ansatz (
3.47
) in the extremal case takes the form

\displaystyle\begin{split}d\hat{s}^{2}\,=&\;\;\frac{1}{\mathcal{Z}_{+}\mathcal{Z}_{-}}dt^{2}-\mathcal{Z}_{0}\mathcal{Z}_{\mathcal{H}}(dr^{2}+r^{2}d\Omega_{(2)}^{2})\\[5.69054pt]
&\,-\ell_{\infty}^{2}\frac{\mathcal{Z}_{0}}{\mathcal{Z}_{\mathcal{H}}}\bigg[dw+\ell_{\infty}^{-1}\beta_{\mathcal{H}}\,q_{\mathcal{H}}\cos\theta d\varphi\bigg]^{2}\\[5.69054pt]
&\,-k_{\infty}^{2}\frac{\mathcal{Z}_{+}}{\mathcal{Z}_{-}}\left[\,dz+k_{\infty}^{-1}\beta_{+}(\mathcal{Z}^{-1}_{+}-1)\,dt\right]^{2}-dy^{\tilde{m}}dy^{\tilde{m}}\,,\end{split}

(3.124a)

\displaystyle\hat{H}\,=

\displaystyle\;\;k_{\infty}\beta_{-}\,d\left[(\mathcal{Z}^{-1}_{-}-1)\,dt\wedge dz\right]+\ell_{\infty}\beta_{0}\,r^{2}\mathcal{Z}_{h0}^{\prime}\,\omega_{(2)}\wedge dw\,,

(3.124b)

\displaystyle e^{-2\hat{\phi}}\,=

\displaystyle\;\;-\frac{c_{\hat{\phi}}}{r^{2}\mathcal{Z}_{-}^{\prime}}\frac{\mathcal{Z}_{-}}{\mathcal{Z}_{0}}\,.

(3.124c)

We use the Zehnbein basis

\displaystyle{\hat{e}}^{0}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{\mathcal{Z}_{+}\mathcal{Z}_{-}}}dt\,,\hskip 19.91684pt{\hat{e}}^{1}=\sqrt{\mathcal{Z}_{0}\mathcal{H}}\,dr\,,\hskip 19.91684pt{\hat{e}}^{2}=\sqrt{\mathcal{Z}_{0}\mathcal{H}}\,rv^{2}\,,\hskip 19.91684pt{\hat{e}}^{3}=\sqrt{\mathcal{Z}_{0}\mathcal{H}}\,rv^{1}\,,

(3.125)

\displaystyle{\hat{e}}^{4}

\displaystyle=\ell_{\infty}\sqrt{\frac{\mathcal{Z}_{0}}{\mathcal{H}}}\left[dw+\beta\ell_{\infty}^{-1}q\cos{\theta}d\varphi\right]\,,

\displaystyle{\hat{e}}^{5}

\displaystyle=k_{\infty}\sqrt{\frac{\mathcal{Z}_{+}}{\mathcal{Z}_{-}}}\left[dz+\beta_{+}k_{\infty}^{-1}\left(\mathcal{Z}^{-1}_{+}-1\right)dt\right]\,,\hskip 19.91684pt{\hat{e}}^{m}=dy^{m}\,,

where

v^{1}=\sin{\theta}d\varphi\,,\hskip 28.45274ptv^{2}=d\theta\,,

(3.126)

are the horizontal components of the left-invariant Maurer-Cartan 1-form of the
SU
(2)/
U
(1)
coset space.

19

19

The details of this construction are
given in appendix
C.5
.

The dilatino Killing spinor equation (KSE) is
\delta_{\epsilon}\lambda=0
,
where the supersymmetry variation of the dilatino with vanishing fermions is
given in Eq. (
3.14
). Substituting the values of the fields, it
can be brought to the form

\left\{\frac{\mathcal{Z}_{0}^{\prime}}{\mathcal{Z}_{0}}\left[1+\beta_{0}\frac{\mathcal{Z}_{h0}^{\prime}}{\mathcal{Z}_{0}^{\prime}}\Gamma^{1234}\right]-\frac{\mathcal{Z}_{-}^{\prime}}{\mathcal{Z}_{-}}\left[1-\beta_{-}\Gamma^{05}\right]+\left(\frac{\mathcal{Z}_{-}^{\prime\prime}}{\mathcal{Z}_{-}^{\prime}}+\frac{2}{r}\right)\right\}\epsilon=0\,.

(3.127)

We can solve this equation without demanding any relations between

\mathcal{Z}_{0}
and
\mathcal{Z}_{-}
if we demand the following two
conditions on the functions:

\displaystyle\mathcal{Z}_{h0}^{\prime}=\mathcal{Z}_{0}^{\prime}\,,\qquad\mathcal{Z}_{-}^{\prime}\propto 1/r^{2}\,,

(3.128a)

which are satisfied by the zeroth-order solutions, and the following
conditions on the Killing spinors:

\displaystyle\left[1+\beta_{0}\Gamma^{1234}\right]\epsilon

\displaystyle=0\,,

(3.129a)

\displaystyle\left[1-\beta_{-}\Gamma^{05}\right]\epsilon

\displaystyle=0\,,

(3.129b)

which are compatible and reduce the number of independent components of the
spinors to a
1/4
of the total 16 real components.

The
a=0
component of the gravitino KSE
\delta_{\epsilon}\psi_{a}=0
,
where the supersymmetry variation of the gravitino with vanishing fermions
is given in Eq. (
3.13
), can be brought to the form

\left\{\partial_{0}-\frac{\left[\log{(\mathcal{Z}_{+}\mathcal{Z}_{-})}\right]^{\prime}}{4(\mathcal{Z}_{0}\mathcal{H})^{1/2}}\left[1-\beta_{-}\frac{\left[\log{(\mathcal{Z}_{+}^{\beta_{+}\beta_{-}}\mathcal{Z}_{-})}\right]^{\prime}}{\left[\log{(\mathcal{Z}_{+}\mathcal{Z}_{-})}\right]^{\prime}}\Gamma^{05}\right]\right\}\epsilon=0\,,

(3.130)

and can be solved by a spinor satisfying
\partial_{0}\epsilon=0
and the condition (
3.129b
) if

\beta_{+}\beta_{-}=+1\,.

(3.131)

The
a=1
component of the gravitino KSE takes the form

\left\{\partial_{r}+\tfrac{1}{4}\beta_{-}\left[\log{(\mathcal{Z}_{+}^{\beta_{+}\beta_{-}}\mathcal{Z}_{-})}\right]^{\prime}\Gamma^{05}\right\}\epsilon=0\,,

(3.132)

and, after use of the conditions (
3.129b
) and (

) it can be
solved by

\epsilon=(\mathcal{Z}_{+}\mathcal{Z}_{-})^{-1/4}\epsilon_{0}\,,

(3.133)

where
\epsilon_{0}
is an
r
-independent spinor that satisfies the conditions
Eqs. (
3.129a
) and (
3.129b
). The
a=4
component takes the form

\left\{\partial_{4}-\tfrac{1}{2}\Gamma^{14}\Omega_{(+)\,414}\left[1+\beta_{0}\frac{\left[\log{(\mathcal{Z}_{0}/\mathcal{H})}\right]^{\prime}}{\left[\frac{\mathcal{Z}_{h0}^{\prime}}{\mathcal{Z}_{0}}+\beta\beta_{0}\frac{q/r^{2}}{\mathcal{H}}\right]}\Gamma^{1234}\right]\right\}\epsilon=0\,,

(3.134)

and, if we demand

\displaystyle\beta\beta_{0}=+1\,,\qquad\mathcal{H}^{\prime}=-q/r^{2}\,,

(3.135a)

it is solved by a spinor satisfying Eq. (
3.129a
) and
\partial_{4}\epsilon=0
. The
a=5
component can be written in the form

\left\{\partial_{5}-\tfrac{1}{2}\Gamma^{01}\Omega_{(+)\,501}\left[1-\beta_{-}\frac{\left[\log{(\mathcal{Z}_{+}/\mathcal{Z}_{-})}\right]^{\prime}}{\left[\log{(\mathcal{Z}_{+}^{\beta_{+}\beta_{-}}/\mathcal{Z}_{-})}\right]^{\prime}}\Gamma^{05}\right]\right\}\epsilon=0\,,

(3.136)

and, if the conditions (
3.131
) and
(
3.129b
) are satisfied, it is solved if, in addition,
\partial_{5}\epsilon=0
.
Finally, using all the conditions derived so far, the
a=2,3
equations can be
combined into a single differential equation for the spinor

\epsilon_{0}=\epsilon_{0}(\theta,\phi)
20
20

20

The last 4 components of the
gravitino KSE are trivially solved by
y
-independent spinors and the
conditions
\partial_{0,4,5}\epsilon=0
imply that
\epsilon_{0}
is
independent of the coordinates
r
,
t
,

,
z
and
y^{i}
.

\left\{d+\tfrac{1}{2}\Gamma^{13}\sin{\theta}d\varphi+\tfrac{1}{2}\Gamma^{12}d\theta+\tfrac{1}{2}\Gamma^{23}\cos{\theta}d\varphi\right\}\epsilon_{0}=0\,.

(3.137)

We can make the following identifications with the generators of the su
(2)

algebra

P_{1}=\tfrac{1}{2}\Gamma^{13}\,,\hskip 28.45274ptP_{2}=\tfrac{1}{2}\Gamma^{12}\,,\hskip 28.45274ptM=\tfrac{1}{2}\Gamma^{23}\,,

(3.138)

because they satisfy the commutation relations Eq. (
C.37
).
Then, using the components of the Maurer-Cartan 1-form in
Eq. (
C.42
), the KSE (
3.137
) can be
rewritten in the form

\left\{d+P_{a}v^{a}+M\vartheta\right\}\epsilon_{0}=0\,.

(3.139)

The 1-forms in this equation can be seen to add up to the left-invariant
Maurer-Cartan 1-form
V=-u^{-1}du
(Eq. (
C.39
)), and the equation
can be rewritten in the form

\left\{d-u^{-1}du\right\}\epsilon_{0}=-u^{-1}d(u\epsilon_{0})=0\,.

(3.140)

Thus, it is solved by

\epsilon_{0}=u^{-1}\epsilon_{00}=e^{(\theta-\pi/2)\tfrac{1}{2}\Gamma^{12}}e^{\varphi\tfrac{1}{2}\Gamma^{13}}\epsilon_{00}\,,

(3.141)

where
\epsilon_{00}
is a constant spinor that satisfies the conditions
(
3.129a
) and (
3.129b
).

Taking into account that we are considering configurations that, at zeroth
order in
\alpha^{\prime}
are determined by the functions

\mathcal{Z}_{i}=1+\frac{q_{i}}{r}\,,\qquad\mathcal{Z}_{h0}=1+\frac{q_{0}}{r}\,,\qquad i=0,\pm,\mathcal{H}

(3.142)

which may have additional corrections at the next
order, we can summarize our results as follows: only the configurations of the
form Eq. (
3.124
) which satisfy all the conditions

\beta_{+}=\beta_{-}\,,\hskip 14.22636pt\beta_{0}=\beta\,,\hskip 14.22636pt\mathcal{Z}_{h0}=\mathcal{Z}_{0}\,,\hskip 14.22636pt\mathcal{Z}_{-}=1+\frac{q_{-}}{r}\,,\hskip 14.22636pt\mathcal{H}=1+\frac{q}{r}\,,

(3.143)

are supersymmetric, and their Killing spinors take the form

\epsilon=(\mathcal{Z}_{+}\mathcal{Z}_{-})^{-1/4}e^{(\theta-\pi/2)\tfrac{1}{2}\Gamma^{12}}e^{\varphi\tfrac{1}{2}\Gamma^{13}}\epsilon_{00}\,,

(3.144)

where the constant spinor
\epsilon_{00}
satisfies

\left[1+\beta_{0}\Gamma^{1234}\right]\epsilon_{00}=0\,,\hskip 28.45274pt\left[1-\beta_{-}\Gamma^{05}\right]\epsilon_{00}=0\,.

(3.145)

3.4.6
4-dimensional, multi-center, 4-charge BHs

Solving the EOMs

We are going solve explicitly the EOMs replacing the ansatz (
3.57
) and assuming that
\mathcal{Z}_{0}
,
\mathcal{Z}_{+}
and
\mathcal{Z}_{-}
are functions in the 4-dimensional Gibbons-Hawking space whose metric is

d\sigma^{2}=\mathcal{Z}_{\mathcal{H}}d\vec{x}_{(3)}^{2}+\ell_{\infty}^{2}\mathcal{Z}_{\mathcal{H}}^{-1}\bigg[dw+\ell_{\infty}^{-1}\beta_{\mathcal{H}}\,\chi\bigg]^{2}\,.

(3.146)

where
\vec{x}_{(3)}
are coordinates of a flat 3-dimensional Euclidean space
\mathbb{E}^{3}
and
\chi
is a 1-form satisfying

d\chi=\star_{(3)}d\mathcal{Z}_{\mathcal{H}}\,.

(3.147)

Notice that (
3.147
) implies that
\mathcal{Z}_{\mathcal{H}}
is a harmonic function of
\mathbb{E}^{3}
and
\chi
is a 1-form of
\mathbb{E}^{3}
. The details of the computations can be found in
[
110
,
2
]
. Some intermediate steps are collected in appendix
C.2
. We recall that the
\beta_{i}
are signs. We restrict ourselves to the case
\beta_{0}\beta_{\mathcal{H}}=1
. The Bianchi identity (
3.12
) is equivalent to

\Delta_{(4)}\left\{\mathcal{Z}_{0}-\frac{\alpha^{\prime}}{4}\left(\frac{\partial_{m}\mathcal{Z}_{0}\,\partial^{m}\mathcal{Z}_{0}}{\mathcal{Z}_{0}^{2}}+\frac{\partial_{m}\mathcal{Z}_{\mathcal{H}}\,\partial^{m}\mathcal{Z}_{\mathcal{H}}}{\mathcal{Z}_{\mathcal{H}}^{2}}\right)\right\}=0\,,

(3.148)

where
\Delta_{(4)}
represents the Laplacian operator on the 4-dimensional Gibbons–Hawking space and
\partial_{m}
are the derivatives dual to the vierbeins
v^{m}
. It is solved by

\mathcal{Z}_{0}=\mathcal{Z}_{0}^{(0)}+\frac{\alpha^{\prime}}{4}\left[\left(\partial\log\mathcal{Z}_{0}^{(0)}\right)^{2}+\left(\partial\log\mathcal{Z}_{\mathcal{H}}^{(0)}\right)^{2}\right]\,,

(3.149)

where
\mathcal{Z}_{0}^{(0)}
is any harmonic function of the Gibbons–Hawking space. Notice that if we further assume that
\mathcal{Z}_{0}^{(0)}
is independent of
w
, then
\mathcal{Z}_{0}^{(0)}
is a harmonic function with respect to the metric
d\sigma^{2}
iff it is a harmonic function in
\mathbb{E}^{3}
. In this way we recover the leading-order solution (
3.60
). The KR equation of motion (
3.11c
) is equivalent to

\Delta_{(4)}\mathcal{Z}_{-}=0\,.

(3.150)

Again, restricting to
\mathcal{Z}_{-}
independent of
w
we obtain

\Delta_{(3)}\mathcal{Z}_{-}=0\,,

(3.151)

where
\Delta_{(3)}
is the Laplacian operator on
\mathbb{E}^{3}
.
We conclude that
\mathcal{Z}_{-}
must be a harmonic function of
\mathbb{E}^{3}
. Finally, we have a single independent EOM among the Einstein equations (
3.11a
) and the dilaton EOM (
3.11b
). The result is the same we got in the case of the 5-dimensional BH. Using the vielbein basis (
C.10
) introduced in appendix
C.2
, the
++
component of Einstein equations reduces to

\Delta_{(4)}\left\{\mathcal{Z}_{+}+\alpha^{\prime}\frac{(1+\beta_{+}\beta_{-})}{4}\left(\frac{\partial_{m}\mathcal{Z}_{+}\,\partial^{m}\mathcal{Z}_{-}}{\mathcal{Z}_{0}\mathcal{Z}_{-}}\right)\right\}=0\,,

(3.152)

which is solved by

\mathcal{Z}_{+}=\mathcal{Z}_{+}^{(0)}-\alpha^{\prime}\frac{(1+\beta_{+}\beta_{-})}{4}\left(\frac{\partial_{m}\mathcal{Z}_{+}^{(0)}\,\partial^{m}\mathcal{Z}_{-}}{\mathcal{Z}_{0}^{(0)}\mathcal{Z}_{-}}\right)\,,

(3.153)

where
\mathcal{Z}_{+}^{(0)}
is a harmonic function of the Gibbons–Hawking space. Assuming that
\mathcal{Z}_{+}^{(0)}
is independent of
w
, we restrict ourselves to the case where
\mathcal{Z}_{+}^{(0)}
is a harmonic function of
\mathbb{E}^{3}
and we connect with the leading-order solution (
3.60
).

Regular solutions

The
\mathcal{Z}_{i}
are determined up to harmonic functions in
\mathbb{E}^{3}
. This harmonic functions can be fixed requiring regularity at the horizon, the proper asymptotic normalization of the fields and selecting the microcanonical ensemble. With the ansatz for the
0^{\rm th}
-order part of
\mathcal{Z}_{i}
given by (
3.60
), we obtain explicitly

\displaystyle\mathcal{Z}_{+}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{Z}_{+}^{(0)}-\frac{\alpha^{\prime}}{4}(1+\beta_{+}\beta_{-})\left[\mathcal{Z}_{0}^{(0)\,-1}\mathcal{Z}_{-}^{-1}\mathcal{Z}_{\mathcal{H}}^{-1}\sum_{a,b}\frac{q^{a}_{+}q^{b}_{-}n_{a}^{m}n_{b}^{m}}{r^{2}_{a}r^{2}_{b}}\right]\,,

(3.154a)

\displaystyle\mathcal{Z}_{0}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{Z}_{0}^{(0)}+\frac{\alpha^{\prime}}{4}\left[\mathcal{Z}_{0}^{(0)\,-2}\mathcal{Z}_{\mathcal{H}}^{-1}\sum_{a,b}\frac{q^{a}_{0}q^{b}_{0}n_{a}^{m}n_{b}^{m}}{r^{2}_{a}r^{2}_{b}}+\mathcal{Z}_{\mathcal{H}}^{-3}\sum_{a,b}\frac{q^{a}_{\mathcal{H}}q^{b}_{\mathcal{H}}n_{a}^{m}n_{b}^{m}}{r^{2}_{a}r^{2}_{b}}\right]\,,

(3.154b)

\displaystyle\mathcal{Z}_{-}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{Z}_{-}^{(0)}\,,

(3.154c)

\displaystyle\mathcal{Z}_{\mathcal{H}}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{Z}_{\mathcal{H}}^{(0)}\,,

(3.154d)

where we have defined the unit radial vectors

n^{m}_{a}\equiv(x^{m}-x^{m}_{a})/r_{a}\,.

(3.155)

3.5
Thermodynamics with
\alpha^{\prime}
corrections

In this section we compute the thermodynamic quantities of the
\alpha^{\prime}
-corrected solutions found in the previous section. We start by explaining how to compute the mass, the temperature, the entropy, the gauge charges, the scalar charges and the chemical potentials. We then apply the formulae to the 5-dimensional, 3-charge and 4-dimensional, 4-charge solutions.

3.5.1
General overview

Mass and scalar charges

The expression for the mass
M
can be obtained by applying the ADM formula. In practice, we can just identify
M
by looking at the asymptotic behavior of the
tt
component of the metric in the modified Einstein frame
[
132
]
,

g_{E}{}_{tt}=e^{-\frac{4}{d-2}(\phi-\phi_{\infty})}g_{tt}\approx 1-\frac{16\pi G_{N}^{(d)}}{(d-2)\omega_{(d-2)}}\frac{M}{r^{d-3}}+\dots\,,

(3.156)

where
\omega_{(d-2)}
is the volume of the unit
{\mathbb{S}}^{(d-2)}
sphere. We recall that

g_{s}^{(d)}\,{}^{2}=g_{s}^{2}\,\text{Vol}_{10-d}/(2\pi\ell_{s})^{n}\,,\qquad G_{N}^{(d)}=G_{N}^{(10)}\,\text{Vol}_{10-d}\,,

(3.157)

with

\text{Vol}(\text{T}^{4})=(2\pi\ell_{s})^{4}\,,\qquad\text{Vol}(\text{S}_{w}^{1})=2\pi\ell_{\infty}\ell_{s}\,,\qquad\text{Vol}(\text{S}_{z}^{1})=2\pi k_{\infty}\ell_{s}\,.

(3.158)

For the scalar fields
\varphi^{x}
normalized in such a way that their kinetic terms have form

\begin{split}\frac{1}{16\pi G_{N}^{(d)}}\int dx^{d}\sqrt{|g_{E}|}\left[\frac{1}{2}g_{xy}\partial_{\mu}\varphi^{x}\partial^{\mu}\varphi^{y}\right]\,,\end{split}

(3.159)

where
g_{xy}
is the scalar metric, we can compute the scalar charges
\Sigma^{x}
via the asymptotic expansion

\phi^{x}\sim\phi_{\infty}^{x}+\frac{4\pi G_{N}^{(d)}}{\omega_{(d-2)}(d-3)}\frac{\Sigma^{x}}{r^{d-3}}+\dots

(3.160)

Temperature

The metric of static spherically-symmetric black holes can always be written as

ds^{2}=g_{tt}(r)dt^{2}-g_{rr}(r)dr^{2}-R(r)^{2}d\Omega_{(d-2)}^{2}\,.

(3.161)

The Hawking temperature is

T_{H}=\frac{1}{4\pi}\frac{\partial_{r}g_{tt}}{\sqrt{|g_{tt}g_{rr}}|}\bigg\rvert_{r=r_{H}}\,,

(3.162)

where
r_{H}
is the position of the outer horizon. It corresponds to the (largest, finite) root of the metric function
g_{tt}
,

g_{tt}(r_{H})=0\,.

(3.163)

Equivalently, we can compute the inverse temperature
\beta
demanding regularity in the Euclidean section
[
134
]
. For the Einstein-frame metrics (
3.30
) and (
3.52
), a simple computation produces

\beta=4\pi\frac{W_{tt}^{\prime}}{\sqrt{h\,W_{tt}W_{rr}^{-1}}}\bigg\rvert_{r=r_{H}}\,,\qquad h=\begin{cases}\mathcal{Z}_{+}\mathcal{Z}_{-}\mathcal{Z}_{0}\,,&\quad d=5\,,\\
\mathcal{Z}_{+}\mathcal{Z}_{-}\mathcal{Z}_{0}\,\mathcal{Z}_{\mathcal{H}}\,,&\quad d=4\,.\end{cases}

(3.164)

Notice that, depending of our choice of boundary conditions, the position of the horizon
r_{H}
may shifted by the
\alpha^{\prime}
corrections.

Gauge charges and dual potentials.

In 5 dimensions we are going to deal with two gauge vectors (KK and winding vectors) carrying electric charges and one rank-2 gauge potential (the 5-dimensional KR field) carrying magnetic charge. Dualizing the KR field we can equivalently work with three electrically charged gauge vectors. In 4 dimensions we have instead four gauge vectors, two of them coming from the dimensional reduction of the metric and two of them coming from the dimensional reduction of the KR field. Two of the four vectors carry electric charge and 2 of them magnetic charge. Again, dualizing the two vectors carrying magnetic charges, we can work with four electrically-charged gauge vectors.

For the computation of the electric and magnetic charges we are going to apply the definitions of section
2.4.2
. Given that we are only interested in the value of the charges and not in the actual construction of the closed currents, we will compute them integrating on a sphere at infinity. In this a way, the only contribution is coming from the currents one would get with the 2-derivative theory.

For the computation of the potentials we follow
2.4.3
. In all cases we have just to determine the electrostatic potential of a gauge vector. Therefore, we have to integrate the momentum-map equation

\iota_{k}F+dP_{k}=0\,,

(3.165)

with
F=dA
a rank 2 form and
P_{k}
a scalar function. To do so, we can profit of the fact that we are considering static configurations with spherical symmetry. In particular, the gauge fields can be written in the static gauge. Then the momentum map is simply

P_{k}=-A_{t}\,,

(3.166)

and the electrostatic potentials are

\Phi_{\mathcal{BH}}=-A_{t}\bigg|_{\mathcal{BH}}\,,\qquad\Phi_{\infty}=-A_{t}\bigg|_{\infty}\,,

(3.167)

In the cases at hand we further have that
A_{t}
vanishes asymptotically. Thus, the potential
\Phi
appearing in the first law is just

\Phi\equiv\Phi_{\mathcal{BH}}-\Phi_{\infty}=\Phi_{\mathcal{BH}}\,.

(3.168)

Black hole entropy.

In higher-derivative theories the entropy can be computed with Wald’s formula
[
61
,
62
]
. However, one of the key assumptions in its derivation does not hold in presence of gravitational Chern-Simons terms, such as the ones present in the heterotic theory.
21
21

21

Namely, that the transformation of the
d
-form Lagrangian
\mathbf{L}
under diffemorphisms is
\delta_{\xi}{\mathbf{L}}={\cal L}_{\xi}{\mathbf{L}}
, being
{\cal L}_{\xi}
the Lie derivative with respect to
\xi
.
As a consequence, different strategies have been proposed in the literature in order to circumvent this issue (see e.g.
[
135
,
125
,
126
,
124
,
136
,
137
]
for a limited list of references), which mainly involve a convenient rewriting of the action. Nevertheless, it is also possible to extend Wald’s formalism to properly account for gravitational Chern-Simons terms. Doing so, general expressions for the black hole entropy were obtained in
[
138
]
and more recently in
[
70
]
. It is more convenient for us to make use of the entropy formula given in
[
70
]
, as it has been derived precisely in the context of the heterotic effective action. We report it here for completeness,

S=\frac{(-1)^{d+1}{\hat{g}}_{s}^{2}}{8\hat{G}_{N}}\int_{\mathcal{BH}}e^{-2\hat{\phi}}\left\{\left[\hat{\star}({\hat{e}}^{\hat{a}}\wedge{\hat{e}}^{\hat{b}})+\frac{\alpha^{\prime}}{2}\star\hat{R}_{(-)}{}^{\hat{a}\hat{b}}\right]{\hat{n}}_{\hat{a}\hat{b}}+(-1)^{d}\frac{\alpha^{\prime}}{2}\Pi_{n}\wedge\hat{\star}{\hat{H}}\right\}\,,

(3.169)

where
\mathcal{BH}
stands for the bifurcation surface of the event horizon and
\hat{R}_{(-)}{}^{\hat{a}\hat{b}}
is the curvature two-form defined in eq. (
3.5a
).
\Pi_{n}
is the vertical Lorentz momentum map associated to the binormal to the Killing horizon,
\hat{n}^{\hat{a}\hat{b}}
, and it is defined by the property

d\Pi_{n}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathcal{BH}}}{{=}}{\hat{R}}_{(-)}{}^{\hat{a}\hat{b}}{\hat{n}}_{\hat{a}\hat{b}}\,.

(3.170)

The formula for the entropy is gauge invariant and frame independent. Performing a local Lorentz transformation we can always put the Vielbein components
\hat{e}_{\hat{\mu}}{}^{\hat{a}}
in an upper triangular form. In such a frame and with our ansatz,
\Pi_{n}
has the explicit expression

\Pi_{n}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathcal{BH}}}{{=}}{\hat{\Omega}}_{(-)}{}^{\hat{a}\hat{b}}{\hat{n}}_{\hat{a}\hat{b}}\,,

(3.171)

and (
3.169
) can be easily evaluated.

3.5.2
Non-extremal, 5-dimensional BHs

Gauge charges, dual potentials and brane sources

Our 5d solution represents a 3-charge BH. More precisely, the BH is electrically charged with respect to the two gauge vectors,
A_{E}
and
C^{(1)}_{E}
, and magnetically charged with respect the Kalb–Ramond field
B_{E}
. Explicitly, we have (all the fields are 5d and in the modified Einstein normalization)

\displaystyle Q_{+}\equiv\frac{1}{16\pi G_{N}^{(5)}}\int_{S^{3}_{\infty}}e^{-4/3\phi}k^{2}\star_{E}F_{E}=\frac{\pi}{4G_{N}^{(5)}}g_{s}^{(5)}{}^{-2/3}k_{\infty}q_{+}\beta_{+}\,,

(3.172a)

\displaystyle Q_{-}\equiv\frac{1}{16\pi G_{N}^{(5)}}\int_{S^{3}_{\infty}}e^{-4/3\phi}k^{-2}\star_{E}G_{E}=\frac{\pi}{4G_{N}^{(5)}}g_{s}^{(5)}{}^{-2/3}k_{\infty}^{-1}q_{-}\beta_{-}\,,

(3.172b)

\displaystyle Q_{0}\equiv-\frac{1}{16\pi G_{N}^{(5)}}\int_{S^{3}_{\infty}}H_{E}=\frac{\pi}{4G_{N}^{(5)}}g_{s}^{(5)}{}^{4/3}q_{0}\beta_{0}\,,

(3.172c)

The normalization of the charges is chosen in such a way that the
Q_{i}
are quantized in units of
1/(\ell_{s}g_{s}^{(5)}{}^{2/3})
. The associated gauge potentials are

\displaystyle\Phi_{+}=\frac{k_{\infty}^{-1}}{\beta_{+}}g_{s}^{2/3}\left(1+\alpha^{\prime}\Delta_{\Phi+}\right)\,,

(3.173a)

\displaystyle\Phi_{-}=\frac{k_{\infty}}{\beta_{-}}g_{s}^{2/3}\left(1+\alpha^{\prime}\Delta_{\Phi-}\right)\,,

(3.173b)

\displaystyle\Phi_{0}=\frac{1}{\beta_{0}}g_{s}^{-4/3}\left(1+\alpha^{\prime}\Delta_{\Phi 0}\right)\,,

(3.173c)

with

\displaystyle\Delta_{\Phi+}

\displaystyle=\frac{\omega\beta_{+}}{2(q_{0}-\omega)(q_{+}-\omega)\beta_{-}}+\frac{3\omega(2q_{0}-3\omega)(2q_{-}-\omega)}{4(q_{0}-\omega)^{2}{D}}\,,

(3.174a)

\displaystyle\Delta_{\Phi-}

\displaystyle=\frac{\omega\beta_{-}}{2(q_{0}-\omega)(q_{-}-\omega)\beta_{+}}+\frac{3\omega(2q_{0}-3\omega)(2q_{+}-\omega)}{4(q_{0}-\omega)^{2}{D}}\,,

(3.174b)

\displaystyle\Delta_{\Phi 0}

\displaystyle=\frac{4q_{-}q_{+}\omega+16q_{0}\omega(q_{-}+q_{+}-\omega)-22\omega^{2}(q_{-}+q_{+})+21\omega^{3}}{4(q_{0}-\omega)^{2}D}\,,

(3.174c)

where
D
is given by

D\equiv 4q_{-}q_{+}-4\omega(q_{0}+q_{-}+q_{+})+4q_{0}(q_{-}+q_{+})+3\omega^{2}\,.

(3.175)

In order to verify the quantization of
Q_{0,-}
we work in 10d HST. We notice that if we have a total of
N_{-}\in\mathbb{Z}
fundamental strings wrapped along the
z
direction and we couple the associated current to the 10d HST effective action, the KR equation takes the form

\frac{g_{s}^{2}}{16\pi G_{N}^{(10)}}\int_{V_{8}}d\left[e^{-2\hat{\phi}}\hat{\star}\hat{H}+\mathcal{O}(\alpha^{\prime}{}^{2})\right]=T_{F1}N_{-}\,,

(3.176)

where
V_{8}
is such that
\partial{V_{8}}=\text{T}^{4}\times\text{S}^{3}_{\infty}
and
T_{F1}=1/2\pi\alpha^{\prime}
. Evaluating the LHS we obtain

\frac{\pi k_{\infty}^{-1}\ell_{s}}{4G_{N}^{(5)}}\frac{1}{2\pi\ell_{s}^{2}}\beta_{-}q_{-}=Q_{-}g_{s}^{(5)}{}^{2/3}\ell_{s}T_{F1}\equiv T_{F1}N_{-}\,.

(3.177)

If we have a total of
N_{0}\in\mathbb{Z}
NS5 branes wrapped along the
z
directions and the internal torus
\text{T}^{4}
and we couple the associated current to the effective action, the Bianchi identity of the KR field takes the form

-\frac{1}{16\pi G_{N}^{(10)}}\int_{V_{4}}dH-\frac{1}{3}T^{(4)}=T_{\text{NS5}}\,N_{0}\,,

(3.178)

with
T_{\text{NS5}}^{-1}=(2\pi\ell_{s})^{5}\ell_{s}g_{s}^{2}
and
V_{4}
the region of spacetime contained in
\partial V_{4}=S_{\infty}^{3}
. We have

\frac{\pi\ell_{s}g_{s}^{(5)}{}^{2}}{4G_{N}^{(5)}}\frac{1}{(2\pi\ell_{s})^{5}\ell_{s}g_{s}^{2}}=Q_{0}g_{s}^{(5)}{}^{2/3}\ell_{s}T_{\text{NS5}}\equiv T_{\text{NS5}}N_{0}\,.

(3.179)

Finally, the quantization of
\mathcal{Q}_{+}
follows using T-duality

Q_{-}\quad\xrightarrow{T_{z}}\quad\frac{k_{\infty}g_{s}^{(5)}{}^{-2/3}}{4G_{N}^{(5)}}\,\beta_{+}\,q_{+}=\mathcal{Q}_{+}=N_{+}\frac{1}{\ell_{s}g_{s}^{(5)}{}^{2/3}}\,,\qquad N_{+}\in\mathbb{Z}\,.

(3.180)

From the point of view of string theory, the solution is a superposition of solitonic 5-branes (NS5) wrapped around the directions parametrized by the coordinates
y^{1},\cdots,y^{4},z
, fundamental strings (F1) wound around the circle parametrized by
z
and waves (W) carrying momentum propagating along the same circle (see table
3.3
). In the extremal case, the integers
N_{i}
have a clear interpretation. Their norm counts the number of sources and their signs distinguish between brane and antibranes. In the non-extremal case, the situation is more subtle and the parameters do not have a clear interpretation. However, it has been proposed in similar settings that they may correspond to the difference between the number of branes and the number of antibranes
[
97
]
.

Table 3.3
:
Sources associated to the five-dimensional black holes. The symbol

\times
stands for the worldvolume directions and
-
for the transverse
directions. The symbol
\sim
denotes a transverse direction over
which the corresponding object has been smeared.

Mass, entropy and temperature

The mass of the BH is

M=\frac{3\pi}{8G_{N}^{(5)}}\left[\frac{2}{3}(q_{+}+q_{-}+q_{0})-\omega\right]

(3.181)

The horizon radius, the Hawking temperature, and the Bekenstein-Hawking and Wald entropies
are given by

\displaystyle R_{H}^{(1)}

\displaystyle=R_{H}^{(0)}\left\{1+\alpha^{\prime}\,\left[\frac{\omega}{8(q_{0}-\omega)^{2}}-\frac{1}{6(q_{0}-\omega)\beta_{+}\beta_{-}}\right]\right\}\,,

(3.182a)

\displaystyle\begin{split}T^{(1)}_{H}&=T^{(0)}_{H}\bigg\{1+\alpha^{\prime}\bigg[\frac{1}{2(q_{0}-\omega)\beta_{-}\beta_{+}}-\frac{8q_{0}^{2}(q_{-}+q_{+}-\omega)+9q_{-}q_{+}\omega}{2(q_{0}-\omega)^{2}D}\\[2.84526pt]
&\hskip 14.22636pt+\frac{4q_{0}\left(4q_{-}q_{+}+11q_{-}\omega+11q_{+}\omega-12\omega^{2}\right)+9\omega^{3}}{8(q_{0}-\omega)^{2}D}\bigg]\bigg\}\,,\end{split}

(3.182b)

\displaystyle S_{BH}^{(1)}

\displaystyle=S_{BH}^{(0)}\left\{1+\alpha^{\prime}\,\left[\frac{3\omega}{8(q_{0}-\omega)^{2}}-\frac{1}{2(q_{0}-\omega)\beta_{+}\beta_{-}}\right]\right\}\,,

(3.182c)

\displaystyle S^{(1)}_{W}

\displaystyle=S^{(0)}_{BH}\left\{1+\alpha^{\prime}\,\left[\frac{8q_{0}-9\omega}{8(q_{0}-\omega)^{2}}+\frac{1}{2(q_{0}-\omega)\beta_{+}\beta_{-}}\right]\right\}\,,

(3.182d)

where
D
is still given by Eq. (
3.175
) and we have the zeroth order quantities

\displaystyle R_{H}^{(0)}

\displaystyle=\left[(q_{+}-\omega)(q_{-}-\omega)(q_{0}-\omega)\right]^{1/6}\,,

(3.183a)

\displaystyle T_{H}^{(0)}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2\pi}\frac{-\omega}{\sqrt{(q_{+}-\omega)(q_{-}-\omega)(q_{0}-\omega)}}\,,

(3.183b)

\displaystyle S_{BH}^{(0)}

\displaystyle=\frac{\pi^{2}}{2G_{N}^{(5)}}\sqrt{(q_{+}-\omega)(q_{-}-\omega)(q_{0}-\omega)}\,.

(3.183c)

First law, smarr formula and scalar charge

We can test if the computed quantities satisfy the first law computing the variations of the gauge charges
Q_{i}
, the entropy
S_{W}
and
S_{BH}
, the mass
M
and the moduli
k_{\infty}
,
\phi_{\infty}
. We assume that
G_{N}^{(5)}
is independent of such variations. We find that only with the Wald entropy (
3.182d
) precisely satisfies

\delta S^{(1)}_{W}=\frac{1}{T^{(1)}_{H}}\bigg[\delta M^{(1)}-\Phi_{i}\delta Q_{i}-{\mathcal{Q}_{k}}\delta k_{\infty}-{\mathcal{Q}_{\ell}}\delta\ell_{\infty}-{\mathcal{Q}_{\phi}}\delta\phi_{\infty}-\Phi^{\alpha^{\prime}}\delta\alpha^{\prime}\bigg]\,.

(3.184)

The coefficients
\mathcal{Q}_{i}
are related to the numerators of the
1/r^{2}
term in the asymptotic expansion of the scalar fields. In particular, given a 2-derivative theory containing Abelian vector fields and scalars coupled to gravity with the scalar kinetic sector

\begin{split}\frac{1}{16\pi G_{N}^{(5)}}\int dx^{5}\sqrt{|g_{E}|}\left[\frac{1}{2}g_{xy}\partial_{\mu}\varphi^{x}\partial^{\mu}\varphi^{y}\right]\,,\end{split}

(3.185)

we obtain (see
[
76
]
)

\mathcal{Q}_{x}=-\frac{1}{4}{g}_{xy}\,\Sigma^{y}\,,

(3.186)

where
\Sigma^{x}
are the scalar charges defined by the asymptotic expansion of the
\varphi^{x}
. In the modified Einstein frame the kinetic term of the scalar sector of the HST action has the form (see appendix
B.3
)

\frac{1}{16\pi G_{N}^{(5)}}\int d^{5}x\sqrt{g_{E}}\bigg[\frac{4}{3}\,(\partial\phi)^{2}+(\partial\log k)^{2}\bigg]\,.

(3.187)

At zeroth order we precisely recover

\displaystyle\mathcal{Q}_{\phi}\,\delta\phi_{\infty}=-\frac{2}{3}\Sigma_{\phi}\,\delta\phi_{\infty}+\mathcal{O}(\alpha^{\prime})\,,

(3.188a)

\displaystyle\mathcal{Q}_{k}\,\delta k_{\infty}=-\frac{1}{2}\Sigma_{\log k}\,\delta\log k_{\infty}+\mathcal{O}(\alpha^{\prime})\,.

(3.188b)

At first order in
\alpha^{\prime}
we should apply the technique of
[
5
]
to HST. This will be the goal of a future work and is beyond the scope of this one
[
139
]
. The explicit forms of the
\mathcal{Q}
s we get are

\displaystyle\mathcal{Q}_{\phi}

\displaystyle=\frac{2}{3}\Phi_{+}\,Q_{+}+\frac{2}{3}\Phi_{-}\,Q_{-}-\frac{4}{3}\Phi_{0}\,Q_{0}\,,

(3.189a)

\displaystyle\mathcal{Q}_{k}

\displaystyle=k_{\infty}^{-1}\left[-\Phi_{+}\,Q_{+}+\Phi_{-}\,Q_{-}\right]\,,

(3.189b)

It is possible to verify that equation (
3.188b
) is still valid at first order in
\alpha^{\prime}
, but (
3.188a
) is not satisfied anymore. If we expand
\delta G_{N}^{(5)}=0
we obtain

\delta\alpha^{\prime}=-\frac{4}{3}\alpha^{\prime}\delta\phi_{\infty}\,.

(3.190)

We can then see that the tern of the first law proportional to
\delta\alpha^{\prime}
is actually contributing to the dilaton charge. We retrieve

\mathcal{Q}_{\phi}\,\delta\phi_{\infty}+\Phi_{\alpha^{\prime}}\delta\alpha^{\prime}=-\frac{2}{3}{\Sigma}_{\phi}\,\delta\phi_{\infty}\,.

(3.191)

The explicit form of the potentials
\Phi_{\alpha^{\prime}}
is

\Phi^{\alpha^{\prime}}=-\frac{\pi\omega}{4G_{N}^{(5)}}\frac{(8q_{0}-9\omega)\beta_{+}\beta_{-}+4(q_{0}-\omega)}{8(q_{0}-\omega)^{2}\beta_{+}\beta_{-}}\,.

(3.192)

Finally, all the quantities satisfy the Smarr formula Eq. (
3.193
)

M^{(1)}=\tfrac{3}{2}S_{W}^{(1)}T_{H}^{(1)}+\Phi^{+}Q_{+}+\Phi^{-}Q_{-}+\Phi^{0}Q_{0}+\Phi^{\alpha^{\prime}}\alpha^{\prime}\,,

(3.193)

3.5.3
Non-extremal, 4-dimensional BHs

Gauge Charges and Brane Sources

Our 4d solution represents a 4-charge BH. More precisely, the BH is electrically charged with respect to two of the gauge vectors,
A^{z}_{E}
and
C^{(1)}_{z\,E}
, and magnetically charged with respect to
A^{w}_{E}
and
C^{(1)}_{w\,E}
. Explicitly, we have (all the fields are 4d and in the modified Einstein normalization)
22
22

22

The expression for the electric charge has been obtained integrating the current
J=\delta(\star\mathcal{L})/\delta F
(which is closed on shell) on the surface at infinity and dropping the terms which are not contributing.

\displaystyle Q_{+}

\displaystyle\equiv\frac{1}{16\pi G_{N}^{(4)}}\int_{S^{2}_{\infty}}e^{-2\phi}k^{2}\star_{E}F^{z}_{E}=\frac{k_{\infty}}{4G_{N}^{(4)}g_{s}^{(4)}}\beta_{+}q_{+}\,,

(3.194a)

\displaystyle Q_{-}

\displaystyle\equiv\frac{1}{16\pi G_{N}^{(4)}}\int_{S^{2}_{\infty}}e^{-2\phi}k^{-2}\star_{E}G_{z\,E}^{(1)}=\frac{k_{\infty}^{-1}}{4G_{N}^{(4)}g_{s}^{(4)}}\beta_{-}q_{-}\,,

(3.194b)

\displaystyle Q_{\mathcal{H}}

\displaystyle\equiv-\frac{1}{16\pi G_{N}^{(4)}}\int_{S^{2}_{\infty}}F^{w}_{E}=\frac{\ell_{\infty}^{-1}g_{s}^{(4)}}{4G_{N}^{(4)}}\,\beta_{\mathcal{H}}\,q\,,

(3.194c)

\displaystyle Q_{0}

\displaystyle\equiv-\frac{1}{16\pi G_{N}^{(4)}}\int_{S^{2}_{\infty}}G_{w\,E}^{(1)}=\frac{\ell_{\infty}g_{s}^{(4)}}{4G_{N}^{(4)}}\,\beta_{0}\,q\,.

(3.194d)

The normalization of the charges is chosen so that the
Q_{i}
have the correct Dirac quantization. With our normalization of the action such condition takes the form (see
[
140
]
or
[
12
]
for a more recent reference)

Q_{i}Q_{j}\in\frac{1}{16\pi G_{N}^{(4)}}2\pi\mathbb{Z}\,.

(3.195)

One can verify that the charges (
3.194
) satisfy (
3.195
) checking that they are quantized in units of
1/(\ell_{s}g_{s}^{(4)})
and recalling that, in our conventions,

\left(\ell_{s}g_{s}^{(4)}\right)^{-2}=\frac{1}{16\pi G_{N}^{(4)}}2\pi\,.

(3.196)

The quantization of
\mathcal{Q}_{\mathcal{H}}
is the simplest to prove. The absence of Dirac-Misner singularities in the 10d ansatz for the metric implies that

N_{\mathcal{H}}=\frac{2\,q\beta_{\mathcal{H}}}{R_{w}}\in\mathbb{Z}\,,

(3.197)

and with a straightforward manipulation we can write the quantized quantity as

N_{\mathcal{H}}=Q_{\mathcal{H}}\ell_{s}g_{s}^{(4)}\,.

(3.198)

The quantization of
Q_{0}
easily follows from the fact that it is the quantity T-dual to
Q_{\mathcal{H}}
. We have indeed

Q_{\mathcal{H}}=\frac{\ell_{\infty}^{-1}g_{s}^{(4)}}{4G_{N}^{(4)}}\,\beta_{\mathcal{H}}\,q\quad\xrightarrow{T_{w}}\quad\frac{\ell_{\infty}g_{s}^{(4)}}{4G_{N}^{(4)}}\,\beta_{0}\,q=\mathcal{Q}_{0}=N_{0}\frac{1}{\ell_{s}g_{s}^{(4)}}\,,\qquad N_{0}\in\mathbb{Z}\,.

(3.199)

In order to verify the quantization of
Q_{-}
we work in 10d HST. We notice that, if we have a total of
N_{-}\in\mathbb{Z}
fundamental strings wrapped along the
z
direction and we couple the associated current to the 10d HST effective action, the KR equation takes the form

\frac{g_{s}^{2}}{16\pi G_{N}^{(10)}}\int_{V_{8}}d\left[e^{-2\hat{\phi}}\hat{\star}\hat{H}+\mathcal{O}(\alpha^{\prime}{}^{2})\right]=T_{F1}N_{-}\,,

(3.200)

where
V_{8}
is such that
\partial{V_{8}}=\text{T}^{4}\times\text{S}^{2}_{\infty}\times\text{S}^{1}_{w}
and
T_{F1}=1/2\pi\alpha^{\prime}
. Evaluating the LHS we obtain

\frac{k_{\infty}^{-1}\ell_{s}}{4G_{N}^{(4)}}\frac{1}{2\pi\ell_{s}^{2}}\beta_{-}q_{-}=Q_{-}g_{s}^{(4)}\ell_{s}T_{F1}\equiv T_{F1}N_{-}\,.

(3.201)

The quantization of
\mathcal{Q}_{+}
follows using T-duality

Q_{-}\quad\xrightarrow{T_{z}}\quad\frac{k_{\infty}}{4G_{N}^{(4)}g_{s}^{(4)}}\,\beta_{+}\,q_{+}=\mathcal{Q}_{+}=N_{+}\frac{1}{\ell_{s}g_{s}^{(4)}}\,,\qquad N_{+}\in\mathbb{Z}\,.

(3.202)

From the point of view of string theory, the solution is a superposition of solitonic 5-branes (NS5) and Kaluza-Klein monopoles (KK6) wrapped around the directions parametrized by the coordinates
y^{1},\cdots,y^{4},z
, fundamental strings (F1) wound around the circle parametrized by
z
and waves (W) carrying momentum propagating along the same circle (see table
3.4
). In the extremal case the integers
N_{i}
have a clear interpretation. Their norm counts the number of sources and their signs distinguish between brane and antibranes. In the non-extremal case the situation is more subtle and the parameters do not have a clear interpretation. However, in similar settings it has been proposed that they may correspond to the difference between the number of branes and the number of antibranes
[
97
]
.

Sources associated to the four-dimensional black holes. The symbol

\times
stands for the worldvolume directions and

for the transverse

Mass, temperature and entropy

The mass of the BH is

M=\frac{1}{4G_{N}^{(4)}}\left[2q+q_{-}+q_{+}-2\omega-(1-s_{0}s_{\mathcal{H}})\frac{\alpha^{\prime}}{5(2q-\omega)}\right]\,.

(3.203)

The Hawking temperature is

T_{H}=\frac{-\omega}{4\pi\sqrt{(q-\omega)^{2}(q_{+}-\omega)(q_{-}-\omega)}}(1+\alpha^{\prime}\Delta_{T})\,,

(3.204)

with

\begin{split}\Delta_{T}=&\frac{q_{-}q_{+}(\beta_{-}\beta_{+}-1)}{8(q-\omega)^{2}(\omega-q_{-})(\omega-q_{+})}+\bigg[4q\omega\left(-5q^{2}-13qq_{-}-13qq_{+}+q_{-}q_{+}\right)\\
&+4q^{2}(5qq_{-}+5qq_{+}+2q_{-}q_{+})+\omega^{3}(-34q-8q_{-}-8q_{+})\\
&+q\omega^{2}(50q+33q_{-}+33q_{+})+8\omega^{4}\bigg]\frac{(s_{0}s_{\mathcal{H}}-1)}{40(q-\omega)^{3}(2q-\omega)D}\\
&+\bigg[\omega^{3}\left(-6q^{2}-19qq_{-}-19qq_{+}+4q_{-}^{2}+26q_{-}q_{+}+4q_{+}^{2}\right)\\
&+\omega^{2}\left(12q^{2}q_{-}+12q^{2}q_{+}+9qq_{-}^{2}+20qq_{-}q_{+}+9qq_{+}^{2}-23q_{-}^{2}q_{+}-23q_{-}q_{+}^{2}\right)\\
&+\omega^{4}(10q-4q_{-}-4q_{+})+\omega\left(20q_{-}^{2}q_{+}^{2}-6q^{2}q_{-}^{2}-16q^{2}q_{-}q_{+}-6q^{2}q_{+}^{2}\right)\\
&+4qq_{-}q_{+}(qq_{-}+qq_{+}-2q_{-}q_{+})\bigg]\frac{1}{8(q-\omega)^{3}(\omega-q_{-})(\omega-q_{+})D}\,,\end{split}

(3.205)

where
D
is the quantity introduced in eq (
3.116
). Finally, the entropy is

S_{W}=\frac{\pi}{G_{N}^{(4)}}\sqrt{(q_{+}-w)(q_{-}-w)\left[(q-w)^{2}+\alpha^{\prime}\Delta_{S}\right]}\,,

(3.206)

with

\Delta_{S}=\frac{5q^{2}-9q\omega+3\omega^{2}}{10q^{2}-15q\omega+5\omega^{2}}+\frac{q\beta_{0}\beta_{\mathcal{H}}(10q^{2}-19q\omega+8\omega^{2})}{20(q-\omega)^{2}(2q-\omega)}+\frac{q_{-}q_{+}\beta_{+}\beta_{-}}{4(q_{+}-\omega)(q_{-}-\omega)}\,.

(3.207)

First law, smarr formula and scalar charges

We want to verify that the thermodynamic quantities we computed satisfy the first law. In order to express the variation of the entropy in term of the physical quantities, we first determine the variation of the mass and charges with respect to the variation of the parameters
q_{i}
,
w
,
k_{\infty}
,
\ell_{\infty}
,
\phi_{\infty}
and
\alpha^{\prime}
, assuming that
G_{N}^{(4)}
is a fixed constant

\displaystyle\delta Q_{0}=\;

\displaystyle-\frac{g_{s}^{(4)}\ell_{\infty}}{4G_{N}^{(4)}}\left[\frac{2q-\omega}{2q\,\beta_{0}}\delta q-\frac{1}{2\beta_{0}}\delta\omega\right]+Q_{0}\,\delta\phi_{\infty}+Q_{0}\,\frac{\delta\ell_{\infty}}{\ell_{\infty}}\,,

(3.208a)

\displaystyle\delta Q_{\mathcal{H}}=\;

\displaystyle-\frac{g_{s}^{(4)}\ell_{\infty}^{-1}}{4G_{N}^{(4)}}\left[\frac{2q-\omega}{2q\,\beta_{\mathcal{H}}}\delta q-\frac{1}{2\beta_{\mathcal{H}}}\delta\omega\right]+Q_{\mathcal{H}}\,\delta\phi_{\infty}-Q_{\mathcal{H}}\,\frac{\delta\ell_{\infty}}{\ell_{\infty}}\,,

(3.208b)

\displaystyle\delta Q_{-}=\;

\displaystyle\frac{k_{\infty}^{-1}}{4G_{N}^{(4)}g_{s}^{(4)}}\left[\frac{2q_{-}-\omega}{2q_{-}\beta_{-}}\delta q_{-}-\frac{1}{2\beta_{-}}\delta\omega\right]-Q_{-}\,\delta\phi_{\infty}-Q_{-}\,\frac{\delta k_{\infty}}{k_{\infty}}\,,

(3.208c)

\displaystyle\delta Q_{+}=\;

\displaystyle\frac{k_{\infty}}{4G_{N}^{(4)}g_{s}^{(4)}}\left[\frac{2q_{+}-\omega}{2q_{+}\beta_{i}}\delta q_{+}-\frac{1}{2\beta_{+}}\delta\omega\right]-Q_{+}\,\delta\phi_{\infty}+Q_{+}\,\frac{\delta k_{\infty}}{k_{\infty}}\,,

(3.208d)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\delta M=\;&\frac{1}{4G_{N}^{(4)}}\bigg\{\left[2+\alpha^{\prime}\frac{2(1-s_{0}s_{\mathcal{H}})}{5(2q-\omega)^{2}}\right]\delta q+\delta q_{-}+\delta q_{+}\\
&+\left[-2+\alpha^{\prime}\frac{(-1+s_{0}s_{\mathcal{H}})}{5(2q-\omega)^{2}}\right]\delta\omega+\frac{(-1+s_{0}s_{\mathcal{H}})}{10q-5\omega}\delta\alpha^{\prime}\bigg\}\,.\end{split}

(3.208e)

Then, we express the variations
\delta q_{i}
and
\delta\omega
in term of the variations of the physical charges and mass. Finally, we replace them in the variation of the entropy expressed in terms of
\delta q_{i}
,
\delta\omega
and
\delta\alpha^{\prime}
. We obtain

\delta S=\frac{1}{T}\left(\delta M-{\Phi_{i}}\,\delta Q_{i}-{\Phi_{\alpha^{\prime}}}\,\delta\alpha^{\prime}-{\mathcal{Q}_{k}}\delta k_{\infty}-{\mathcal{Q}_{\ell}}\delta\ell_{\infty}-{\mathcal{Q}_{\phi}}\delta\phi_{\infty}\right)\,.

(3.209)

The temperature
T
appearing in the first law matches the Hawking temperature (
3.204
), providing an highly non-trivial check that the gauge invariant entropy formula proposed by
[
70
]
is the correct one to use (repeating the same computation with the standard Iyer–Wald prescription we do not recover the Hawking temperature).

The coefficients
\Phi_{\pm}
match the electrostatic potentials of
[
70
]
, with the subtlety that they must be computed with the fields in the modified Einstein normalization (it will be relevant in order to have a canonically normalized scalar charge). They are defined by

\Phi_{+}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathcal{BH}}}{{=}}-\iota_{t}A^{z}_{E}\,,\qquad\Phi_{-}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathcal{BH}}}{{=}}-\iota_{t}C^{(1)}_{z\,E}\,.

(3.210)

The coefficients
\Phi_{0,\mathcal{H}}
match the magnetic potentials of
[
73
]
. They are defined as the electrostatic potential of the dual gauge fields. Again, they must be computed with the fields in the modified Einstein normalization

\Phi_{\mathcal{H}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathcal{BH}}}{{=}}-\iota_{t}A^{w}_{E}{}^{D}\,,\qquad\Phi_{0}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathcal{BH}}}{{=}}-\iota_{t}C^{(1)}{}^{D}_{\omega\,E}\,.

(3.211)

The simplest way to compute them is evaluating first
\Phi_{0}
and then obtaining
\Phi_{\mathcal{H}}
performing a T-duality transformation. In order to compute
\Phi_{0}
we could dualize directly
C^{(1)}{}^{D}_{\omega}
. However, it is simpler to perform the dualization in 10d and then perform a dimensional reduction. Therefore, we start dualizing the KR field directly in in 10d

\hat{H}^{(7)}=e^{-2\hat{\phi}}\hat{\star}\,\hat{H}\,.

(3.212)

We have, then
23
23

23

The dimensional reduction is straightforward because now the Bianchi identity is just
d\mathcal{H}^{(7)}=0
and the only non-vanishing components are those with the form
\mathcal{H}^{(7)}_{\mu\nu my^{1}\dots y^{4}}
. In particular, in the relation between higher- and lower-dimensional fields there are no explicit
\alpha^{\prime}
corrections.

G^{(1)}{}^{D}_{\omega}=\frac{1}{2}\hat{H}^{(7)}_{\mu\nu\bar{z}y^{1}\dots y^{4}}\,dx^{\mu}\wedge dx^{\nu}\,,

(3.213)

and the modified Einstein frame field strength

G^{(1)}_{w\,E}{}^{D}=G^{(1)}_{w}{}^{D}\,e^{\phi_{\infty}}\,.

(3.214)

The explicit expressions of the potentials are

\displaystyle\Phi_{+}

\displaystyle=\frac{k_{\infty}^{-1}g_{s}^{(4)}}{\beta_{+}}\left(1+\alpha^{\prime}\Delta_{\Phi+}\right)\,,

(3.215a)

\displaystyle\Phi_{-}

\displaystyle=\frac{k_{\infty}g_{s}^{(4)}}{\beta_{-}}\left(1+\alpha^{\prime}\Delta_{\Phi-}\right)\,,

(3.215b)

\displaystyle\Phi_{0}

\displaystyle=\frac{\ell_{\infty}^{-1}}{g_{s}^{(4)}\,\beta_{0}}\left(1+\alpha^{\prime}\Delta_{\Phi 0}\right)\,,

(3.215c)

\displaystyle\Phi_{\mathcal{H}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\ell_{\infty}}{g_{s}^{(4)}\,\beta_{\mathcal{H}}}\left(1+\alpha^{\prime}\Delta_{\Phi\mathcal{H}}\right)\,,

(3.215d)

with

\displaystyle\begin{split}\Delta_{\Phi+}=&\;\frac{\omega(q-2\omega)(\omega-2q_{-})(s_{0}s_{\mathcal{H}}+4)}{10(q-\omega)^{3}D}+\frac{\beta_{-}\beta_{+}q_{-}\omega}{8(q-\omega)^{2}(q_{-}-\omega)(q_{+}-\omega)}\,,\end{split}

(3.216a)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\Delta_{\Phi-}=&\;\frac{\omega(q-2\omega)(\omega-2q_{+})(s_{0}s_{\mathcal{H}}+4)}{10(q-\omega)^{3}D}+\frac{\beta_{-}\beta_{+}q_{+}\omega}{8(q-\omega)^{2}(q_{-}-\omega)(q_{+}-\omega)}\,,\end{split}

(3.216b)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\Delta_{\Phi 0}=&\;s_{0}s_{\mathcal{H}}\bigg[4\omega^{2}\left(3q^{2}+qq_{-}+qq_{+}+3q_{-}q_{+}\right)\\
&-8q\omega\left(q^{2}+2qq_{-}+2qq_{+}+3q_{-}q_{+}\right)+8q^{2}(qq_{-}+qq_{+}+2q_{-}q_{+})\\
&+\omega^{3}(2q-q_{-}-q_{+})-2\omega^{4}\bigg]\frac{1}{40(q-\omega)^{3}(2q-\omega)D}\\
&+\bigg[-8\omega^{2}\left(9q^{2}+18qq_{-}+18qq_{+}+4q_{-}q_{+}\right)\\
&+4q\omega\left(2q^{2}+19qq_{-}+19qq_{+}+16q_{-}q_{+}\right)-8q^{2}(qq_{-}+qq_{+}+2q_{-}q_{+})\\
&+8\omega^{3}(16q+7q_{-}+7q_{+})-48\omega^{4}\bigg]\frac{1}{40(q-\omega)^{3}(2q-\omega)D}\,,\end{split}

(3.216c)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\Delta_{\Phi\mathcal{H}}=&\;\Delta_{\Phi 0}\,,\end{split}

(3.216d)

where
D
is the quantity defined in equation (
3.116
).

Let us consider now the coefficients of the variations of the moduli. The explicit form of the
\mathcal{Q}
s we get is

\displaystyle\mathcal{Q}_{\phi}

\displaystyle=\Phi_{+}\,Q_{+}+\Phi_{-}\,Q_{-}-\Phi_{0}\,Q_{0}-\Phi_{\mathcal{H}}\,Q_{\mathcal{H}}\,,

(3.217a)

\displaystyle\mathcal{Q}_{k}

\displaystyle=k_{\infty}^{-1}\left[-\Phi_{+}\,Q_{+}+\Phi_{-}\,Q_{-}\right]\,,

(3.217b)

\displaystyle\mathcal{Q}_{\ell}

\displaystyle=\ell_{\infty}^{-1}\left[-\Phi_{0}\,Q_{0}+\Phi_{\mathcal{H}}\,Q_{\mathcal{H}}\right]\equiv 0\,.

(3.217c)

If we expand
\delta G_{N}^{(4)}=0
, we obtain

\delta\alpha^{\prime}=-2\alpha^{\prime}\delta\phi_{\infty}\,.

(3.218)

The potential
\Phi_{\alpha}
contributes to the dilaton charge. Indeed, we recover

\displaystyle\mathcal{Q}_{\phi}\,\delta\phi_{\infty}=-{\Sigma}_{\phi}\,\delta\phi_{\infty}-\Phi_{\alpha^{\prime}}\delta\alpha^{\prime}\,,

(3.219a)

\displaystyle\mathcal{Q}_{k}\,\delta k_{\infty}=-\frac{1}{2}\Sigma_{\log k}\,\delta\log k_{\infty}\,,

(3.219b)

\displaystyle\mathcal{Q}_{\ell}\,\delta\ell_{\infty}=-\frac{1}{2}\Sigma_{\log\ell}\,\delta\log\ell_{\infty}\,.

(3.219c)

Finally, the explicit expression of the potential
\Phi_{\alpha^{\prime}}
is

\begin{split}\Phi_{\alpha^{\prime}}=&\;\frac{1}{G_{N}^{(4)}}\bigg[\frac{4q^{2}(-1+s_{0}s_{\mathcal{H}})-5q(-4+s_{0}s_{\mathcal{H}})\omega-20\omega^{2}}{160(q-\omega)^{3}}\\
&+\frac{5q_{-}q_{+}(q-\omega)\omega\beta_{-}\beta_{+}}{160(q-\omega)^{3}(q_{-}-\omega)(q_{+}-\omega)}\bigg]\,,\end{split}

(3.220)

and it is exactly the one which allows the Smarr formula to be satisfied
24
24

24

The presence of a potential in the Smarr formula is expected for every independent dimensionful parameter. See for instance
[
2
,
72
,
71
]
.

M=2S_{W}T_{H}+\Phi_{i}Q_{i}+2\Phi_{\alpha^{\prime}}\,\alpha^{\prime}\,.

(3.221)

3.5.4
Extremal BHs and Weak Gravity Conjecture

Thermodynamics

The thermodynamic quantities can be easily obtained with the extremal limit
\omega=0
. The temperature vanishes

T_{H}=0\,.

(3.222)

The gauge charges are in 5d

\displaystyle Q_{+}=\frac{\pi}{4G_{N}^{(5)}}g_{s}^{(5)}{}^{-2/3}k_{\infty}q_{+}\beta_{+}=N_{+}/\ell_{q}\,,

(3.223a)

\displaystyle Q_{-}=\frac{\pi}{4G_{N}^{(5)}}g_{s}^{(5)}{}^{-2/3}k_{\infty}^{-1}q_{-}\beta_{-}=N_{-}/\ell_{q}\,,

(3.223b)

\displaystyle Q_{0}=\frac{\pi}{4G_{N}^{(5)}}g_{s}^{(5)}{}^{4/3}q_{0}\beta_{0}=N_{0}/\ell_{q}\,,

(3.223c)

and in 4d

\displaystyle Q_{+}

\displaystyle=\frac{k_{\infty}}{4G_{N}^{(4)}}\left(g_{s}^{(4)}\right)^{-1}\beta_{+}q_{+}=N_{+}/\ell_{q}\,,

(3.224a)

\displaystyle Q_{-}

\displaystyle=\frac{k_{\infty}^{-1}}{4G_{N}^{(4)}}\left(g_{s}^{(4)}\right)^{-1}\beta_{-}q_{-}=N_{-}/\ell_{q}\,,

(3.224b)

\displaystyle Q_{\mathcal{H}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\ell_{\infty}^{-1}}{4G_{N}^{(4)}}g_{s}^{(4)}\,\beta_{\mathcal{H}}\,q_{\mathcal{H}}=N_{\mathcal{H}}/\ell_{q}\,,

(3.224c)

\displaystyle Q_{0}

\displaystyle=\frac{\ell_{\infty}}{4G_{N}^{(4)}}g_{s}^{(4)}\,\beta_{0}\,q_{0}=N_{0}/\ell_{q}\,,

(3.224d)

with
N_{i}\in\mathbb{Z}
and
\beta_{i}^{2}=1
. The parameter
\ell_{q}
is defined as

\ell_{q}=\left(g_{s}^{(d)}\right)^{\frac{2}{d-2}}\ell_{s}\,.

(3.225)

Once we replace in the entropy the physical charges we obtain

S_{W}=2\pi\sqrt{|N_{+}N_{-}|(k+2+\beta_{+}\beta_{-})}\,,

(3.226)

with

k=\begin{cases}|N_{0}|\,,&\quad\text{if}\,d=5\,.\\[2.84526pt]
|N_{0}N_{\mathcal{H}}|+\beta_{0}\beta_{\mathcal{H}}\,,&\quad\text{if}\,d=4\,.\end{cases}

(3.227)

The mass is

M=\frac{k_{\infty}}{\ell_{s}}|N_{-}|+\frac{1}{\ell_{s}k_{\infty}}|N_{+}|+\frac{1}{\ell_{s}\ell_{\infty}g_{s}^{(d)}{}^{2}}|N_{0}|+\frac{\ell_{\infty}}{\ell_{s}g_{s}^{(d)}{}^{2}}|N_{\mathcal{H}}|+\delta M\,,

(3.228)

with
N_{\mathcal{H}}=0
and
\ell_{\infty}=1
in
d=5
. The mass shift
\delta M
reads

\delta M=(d-5)\frac{(1-\beta_{0}\beta_{\mathcal{H}})}{2}\frac{16\ell_{\infty}}{\ell_{s}g_{s}^{(d)}{}^{2}|N_{0}|}f(v)\,,

(3.229)

Notice that
\delta M\neq 0
only in
d=4
for non-supersymmetric solutions such that
\beta_{0}\beta_{\mathcal{H}}=-1
.
f(v)
is defined as

f(v)=\frac{v^{4}-8v^{3}+8v-1+12v^{2}\log{\left(v\right)}}{(v-1)^{5}}\,,\quad v=\frac{|N_{\mathcal{H}}|}{|N_{0}|}\ell_{\infty}^{2}\,.

(3.230)

Notice that despite
the apparent singularity of
f(v)
for
v=1
, this function is actually smooth
for every
v\geq 0
, as illustrated in Fig.
3.7
. In fact, we have
\lim_{v\rightarrow 1}f(v)=2/5
.

Weak Gravity Conjecture

Higher-derivative corrections to the mass of extremal black holes are a matter of interest in the context of the Weak Gravity Conjecture

[
141
,
142
,
143
,
144
,
145
,
146
,
112
,
147
,
148
,
149
,
150
,
151
]
. Originally,
this conjecture has been formulated for black holes charged under a single

U(1)
field, and it states that, in a consistent theory of Quantum Gravity,
